## I The Millionaire and the Waiter / I 百万富翁与侍者

===Original===
“Yes, sir?”

Jules, the celebrated head waiter of the Grand Babylon, was bending formally towards the alert, middle-aged man who had just entered the smoking-room and dropped into a basket-chair in the corner by the conservatory. It was 7:45 on a particularly sultry June night, and dinner was about to be served at the Grand Babylon. Men of all sizes, ages, and nationalities, but everyone alike arrayed in faultless evening dress, were dotted about the large, dim apartment. A faint odour of flowers came from the conservatory, and the tinkle of a fountain. The waiters, commanded by Jules, moved softly across the thick Oriental rugs, balancing their trays with the dexterity of jugglers, and receiving and executing orders with that air of profound importance of which only really first-class waiters have the secret. The atmosphere was an atmosphere of serenity and repose, characteristic of the Grand Babylon. It seemed impossible that anything could occur to mar the peaceful, aristocratic monotony of existence in that perfectly-managed establishment. Yet on that night was to happen the mightiest upheaval that the Grand Babylon had ever known.

“Yes, sir?” repeated Jules, and this time there was a shade of august disapproval in his voice: it was not usual for him to have to address a customer twice.

“Oh!” said the alert, middle-aged man, looking up at length. Beautifully ignorant of the identity of the great Jules, he allowed his grey eyes to twinkle as he caught sight of the expression on the waiter’s face. “Bring me an Angel Kiss.”

===Chinese===
“您吩咐，先生？”

大巴比伦饭店（the Grand Babylon）大名鼎鼎的领班朱尔（Jules），正客客气气地欠着身，对着刚走进吸烟室的那位精明干练的中年男士——后者一屁股坐进了花房角落里的一张藤椅。时值六月一个分外闷热的夜晚，7 点 45 分，大巴比伦饭店即将开晚饭。这间宽敞幽暗的大厅里，星罗棋布地坐着高矮胖瘦、年岁国籍各异的客人，人人却一律穿着无可挑剔的晚礼服。花房送来一缕淡淡的花香，其间夹着喷泉的叮咚声。侍者们由朱尔统带，踏着厚厚的东方地毯悄声穿行，托盘端得同杂耍艺人一般稳，接令、传令时那副煞有介事的神气，唯有真正头等的侍者才懂得其中的窍门。四下里一派宁静安详——这正是大巴比伦独有的气象。在这座经营得尽善尽美的邸馆里，似乎不可能出什么乱子，搅扰它那贵族气派、四平八稳的日常。然而就在这一夜，大巴比伦有史以来最惊天动地的一场剧变，即将发生。

“您吩咐，先生？”朱尔又说了一遍，这一回话音里透出一丝威严的不悦：让客人逼得自己开口两遍，可不是他素来的规矩。

“哦！”那位精明干练的中年男士这才抬起头来。他对大名鼎鼎的朱尔是何许人一无所知，而且一无所知得理直气壮；一眼瞧见侍者脸上的神色，他那双灰眼睛不由得闪了闪。“给我来一杯天使之吻（Angel Kiss）。”

===Original===
“Pardon, sir?”

“Bring me an Angel Kiss, and be good enough to lose no time.”

“If it’s an American drink, I fear we don’t keep it, sir.” The voice of Jules fell icily distinct, and several men glanced round uneasily, as if to deprecate the slightest disturbance of their calm. The appearance of the person to whom Jules was speaking, however, reassured them somewhat, for he had all the look of that expert, the travelled Englishman, who can differentiate between one hotel and another by instinct, and who knows at once where he may make a fuss with propriety, and where it is advisable to behave exactly as at the club. The Grand Babylon was a hotel in whose smoking-room one behaved as though one was at one’s club.

“I didn’t suppose you did keep it, but you can mix it, I guess, even in this hotel.”

“This isn’t an American hotel, sir.” The calculated insolence of the words was cleverly masked beneath an accent of humble submission.

===Chinese===
“您说什么，先生？”

“给我来一杯天使之吻，劳驾别磨蹭。”

“倘若是美国酒，本店恐怕不备，先生。”朱尔的声音冷冰冰的，字字分明；好几个人不安地回过头来，仿佛连一丝一毫的搅扰都嫌多余。不过，看清朱尔交谈的对象之后，他们又稍稍放了心：此人通身的气派，分明是那种行家——游历广博的英国绅士，凭本能便分得出饭店的高下，并且立刻知道在什么地方尽可以合乎体统地摆一摆谱，在什么地方最好拿出俱乐部里的规矩来。大巴比伦便是这样一家饭店：在它的吸烟室里，人人举止如在自己的俱乐部。

“我原就没指望你们备着。不过我猜，就算是这家饭店，总也调得出一杯来吧。”

“本店可不是美国饭店，先生。”这话字字冒犯、处心积虑，却巧妙地裹在一层卑顺谦恭的声口底下。

===Original===
The alert, middle-aged man sat up straight, and gazed placidly at Jules, who was pulling his famous red side-whiskers.

“Get a liqueur glass,” he said, half curtly and half with good-humoured tolerance, “pour into it equal quantities of maraschino, cream, and crême de menthe. Don’t stir it; don’t shake it. Bring it to me. And, I say, tell the bartender—”

“Bartender, sir?”

“Tell the bartender to make a note of the recipe, as I shall probably want an Angel Kiss every evening before dinner so long as this weather lasts.”

“I will send the drink to you, sir,” said Jules distantly. That was his parting shot, by which he indicated that he was not as other waiters are, and that any person who treated him with disrespect did so at his own peril.

===Chinese===
精明干练的中年男士直起身子，心平气和地打量着朱尔；朱尔正捻着他那把大名鼎鼎的红色连鬓胡。

“取一只利口酒杯来，”他说，口气一半生硬，一半带着好脾气的宽容，“把黑樱桃利口酒（maraschino）、奶油和薄荷利口酒（crême de menthe）等量倒进去。不要搅，不要摇。给我端来。对了，我说，叫你们调酒师——”

“调酒师，先生？”

“叫调酒师把这个配方记下来。只要这种天气持续一天，我多半每晚晚饭前都要来一杯天使之吻。”

“酒会送去给您，先生。”朱尔淡淡的，疏远得很。这是他的临别一枪，意在表明：他朱尔不是寻常侍者可比，谁若对他失礼，后果自负。

===Original===
A few minutes later, while the alert, middle-aged man was tasting the Angel Kiss, Jules sat in conclave with Miss Spencer, who had charge of the bureau of the Grand Babylon. This bureau was a fairly large chamber, with two sliding glass partitions which overlooked the entrance-hall and the smoking-room. Only a small portion of the clerical work of the great hotel was performed there. The place served chiefly as the lair of Miss Spencer, who was as well known and as important as Jules himself. Most modern hotels have a male clerk to superintend the bureau. But the Grand Babylon went its own way. Miss Spencer had been bureau clerk almost since the Grand Babylon had first raised its massive chimneys to heaven, and she remained in her place despite the vagaries of other hotels. Always admirably dressed in plain black silk, with a small diamond brooch, immaculate wristbands, and frizzed yellow hair, she looked now just as she had looked an indefinite number of years ago. Her age—none knew it, save herself and perhaps one other, and none cared. The gracious and alluring contours of her figure were irreproachable; and in the evenings she was a useful ornament of which any hotel might be innocently proud. Her knowledge of Bradshaw, of steamship services, and the programmes of theatres and music-halls was unrivalled; yet she never travelled, she never went to a theatre or a music-hall. She seemed to spend the whole of her life in that official lair of hers, imparting information to guests, telephoning to the various departments, or engaged in intimate conversations with her special friends on the staff, as at present.

“Who’s Number 107?” Jules asked this black-robed lady.

Miss Spencer examined her ledgers.

“Mr. Theodore Racksole, New York.”

“I thought he must be a New Yorker,” said Jules, after a brief, significant pause, “but he talks as good English as you or me. Says he wants an ‘Angel Kiss’—maraschino and cream, if you please—every night. I’ll see he doesn’t stop here too long.”

===Chinese===
几分钟之后，正当那位精明干练的中年男士品尝天使之吻的当口，朱尔已同斯宾塞小姐（Miss Spencer）密谈起来。斯宾塞小姐执掌大巴比伦的事务处。事务处是一间相当宽敞的房室，两道活动玻璃隔断俯临门厅与吸烟室。这家大饭店的文书事务，真正在此办理的不过一小部分；这地方主要是斯宾塞小姐的巢穴，而她的名望与分量，同朱尔不相上下。如今大多数饭店都派男职员掌管事务处，大巴比伦却自行其是。几乎打从大巴比伦头一回把粗大的烟囱竖向天际起，斯宾塞小姐就做着事务处文员；任凭别家饭店的风气怎么翻新，她的位子岿然不动。她永远一身素净的黑绸衣裙，穿戴着体面之极：一枚小小的钻石胸针，雪白挺括的腕口，烫得一丝不苟的黄色鬈发；如今这副模样，同不知多少年前相比毫无二致。她的年纪——除了她自己，兴许还有一个人，谁也不知道，谁也不在乎。她体态丰盈妩媚，线条无可指摘；到了晚间，她便是一件有用的装饰，任何饭店尽可以问心无愧地引以为荣。论《布拉德肖》铁路时刻表（Bradshaw）、轮船班次、剧院与音乐厅的节目单，她的学问举世无双；可她自己从不旅行，从不上剧院或音乐厅。她仿佛把一辈子都消磨在那间公务巢穴里：给客人答疑，往各部门打电话，或者——就像眼下这样——同她在员工里的几位密友促膝交谈。

“107 号房住的是谁？”朱尔问这位黑衣女士。

斯宾塞小姐翻查她的登记簿。

“西奥多·拉克索尔（Theodore Racksole）先生，纽约。”

“我就说嘛，他准是个纽约客，”朱尔停了短短一停，意味深长地说，“不过他那口英语，说得同你我不差上下。说什么每天晚上都要来一杯‘天使之吻’——您听听，黑樱桃利口酒加奶油！我倒要盯着点儿，别让他在本店住得太久。”

===Original===
Miss Spencer smiled grimly in response. The notion of referring to Theodore Racksole as a “New Yorker” appealed to her sense of humour, a sense in which she was not entirely deficient. She knew, of course, and she knew that Jules knew, that this Theodore Racksole must be the unique and only Theodore Racksole, the third richest man in the United States, and therefore probably in the world. Nevertheless she ranged herself at once on the side of Jules.

Just as there was only one Racksole, so there was only one Jules, and Miss Spencer instinctively shared the latter’s indignation at the spectacle of any person whatsoever, millionaire or Emperor, presuming to demand an “Angel Kiss,” that unrespectable concoction of maraschino and cream, within the precincts of the Grand Babylon. In the world of hotels it was currently stated that, next to the proprietor, there were three gods at the Grand Babylon—Jules, the head waiter, Miss Spencer, and, most powerful of all, Rocco, the renowned chef, who earned two thousand a year, and had a chalet on the Lake of Lucerne. All the great hotels in Northumberland Avenue and on the Thames Embankment had tried to get Rocco away from the Grand Babylon, but without success. Rocco was well aware that even he could rise no higher than the maître d’hôtel of the Grand Babylon, which, though it never advertised itself, and didn’t belong to a limited company, stood an easy first among the hotels of Europe—first in expensiveness, first in exclusiveness, first in that mysterious quality known as “style.”

Situated on the Embankment, the Grand Babylon, despite its noble proportions, was somewhat dwarfed by several colossal neighbours. It had but three hundred and fifty rooms, whereas there are two hotels within a quarter of a mile with six hundred and four hundred rooms respectively. On the other hand, the Grand Babylon was the only hotel in London with a genuine separate entrance for Royal visitors constantly in use. The Grand Babylon counted that day wasted on which it did not entertain, at the lowest, a German prince or the Maharajah of some Indian State. When Félix Babylon—after whom, and not with any reference to London’s nickname, the hotel was christened—when Félix Babylon founded the hotel in 1869 he had set himself to cater for Royalty, and that was the secret of his triumphant eminence.

===Chinese===
斯宾塞小姐冷冷一笑，权作回答。把西奥多·拉克索尔称作“纽约客”，这个念头正搔着她的幽默痒处——这份幽默，她倒也不怎么匮乏。她自然心知肚明，也知道朱尔同样心里有数：这位西奥多·拉克索尔，必是如假包换、独一无二的那一位，合众国第三的富翁，因而多半也是全世界第三的富翁。尽管如此，她仍旧立刻站到了朱尔那一边。

正如拉克索尔只有一个，朱尔也只有一个；凡是眼见有人——管他是百万富翁还是皇帝——竟敢在大巴比伦的辖境之内点叫“天使之吻”这种不登大雅的黑樱桃利口酒加奶油的混调玩意儿，斯宾塞小姐都会本能地同朱尔一般义愤填膺。饭店这一行里向来流传：大巴比伦除老板之外，还供着三尊神明——领班朱尔，斯宾塞小姐，还有权势最盛的名厨罗科（Rocco）：此人年薪两千英镑，在卢塞恩湖畔还置着一座木屋别墅。诺森伯兰大道上、泰晤士河堤沿线的各大饭店，都曾打主意把罗科从大巴比伦挖走，无一得逞。罗科心里雪亮：就算是他这等人物，再往上攀，也高不过大巴比伦总管（maître d’hôtel）的名分；而大巴比伦虽从不自我宣扬，也不属于任何股份有限公司，在欧洲饭店中却稳稳当当居于首位——论昂贵第一，论不纳俗客第一，论那一样唤作“气派”的神秘品质，也是第一。

大巴比伦坐落在河堤（the Embankment）上，气象虽然尊贵，在左近几座庞然大物的映衬之下却不免矮了一头。它只有 350 间客房；而四分之一英里之内，便有两家饭店分别坐拥 600 间与 400 间客房。反过来说，全伦敦只有大巴比伦一家饭店，有一条真正专供王室贵客、并且常年启用的独立门。大巴比伦的算盘是：哪一天若没有款待起码一位德意志王子，或者某位印度土邦的王公（Maharajah），那一天便算虚度。费利克斯·巴比伦（Félix Babylon）——饭店即以他命名，与伦敦的那个绰号并无干系——1869 年他创立这座饭店时，便立志专事供奉王室，这正是他煊赫腾达的全部秘诀。

===Original===
The son of a rich Swiss hotel proprietor and financier, he had contrived to establish a connection with the officials of several European Courts, and he had not spared money in that respect. Sundry kings and not a few princesses called him Félix, and spoke familiarly of the hotel as “Félix’s”; and Félix had found that this was very good for trade. The Grand Babylon was managed accordingly. The “note” of its policy was discretion, always discretion, and quietude, simplicity, remoteness. The place was like a palace incognito. There was no gold sign over the roof, not even an explanatory word at the entrance. You walked down a small side street off the Strand, you saw a plain brown building in front of you, with two mahogany swing doors, and an official behind each; the doors opened noiselessly; you entered; you were in Félix’s. If you meant to be a guest, you, or your courier, gave your card to Miss Spencer. Upon no consideration did you ask for the tariff. It was not good form to mention prices at the Grand Babylon; the prices were enormous, but you never mentioned them. At the conclusion of your stay a bill was presented, brief and void of dry details, and you paid it without a word. You met with a stately civility, that was all. No one had originally asked you to come; no one expressed the hope that you would come again. The Grand Babylon was far above such manoeuvres; it defied competition by ignoring it; and consequently was nearly always full during the season.

If there was one thing more than another that annoyed the Grand Babylon—put its back up, so to speak—it was to be compared with, or to be mistaken for, an American hotel. The Grand Babylon was resolutely opposed to American methods of eating, drinking, and lodging—but especially American methods of drinking. The resentment of Jules, on being requested to supply Mr. Theodore Racksole with an Angel Kiss, will therefore be appreciated.

“Anybody with Mr. Theodore Racksole?” asked Jules, continuing his conversation with Miss Spencer. He put a scornful stress on every syllable of the guest’s name.

“Miss Racksole—she’s in No. 111.”

===Chinese===
他是瑞士一位富有的饭店老板兼金融家的儿子，居然设法同好几处欧洲宫廷的官员搭上了关系，这方面花起钱来毫不吝惜。好些国王和不少公主直呼他“费利克斯”，提起这家饭店也亲亲热热地叫它“费利克斯家”；费利克斯发现，这买卖上的好处着实不小。大巴比伦的经营管理便照此而定：其方略的“基调”是审慎，永远是审慎，外加清静、简约、避世。这地方像一座隐姓埋名的宫殿。屋顶上没有金字招牌，入口处连一句说明的话也没有。你从河岸街（Strand）旁的一条小横街走过去，眼前是一幢朴素的褐色房子，两扇红木弹簧门，每扇门后立着一名门房；门悄然无声地开了；你走进去；你便置身“费利克斯家”。倘若你真有意住下，你——或者你的旅行侍从——把名片递给斯宾塞小姐便是。你无论如何也不会去讨价目表。在大巴比伦，提价钱有失体统；价钱固然大得吓人，可你从不提它。住到临别，账单送来，简短干净，没有半点枯燥琐碎的明细，你一言不发付清便是。你所得的不过是一种雍容得体的礼数，仅此而已。当初没有人请你来，临了也没有人盼你再来。大巴比伦远远高出这类手腕；它以视若无睹来蔑视竞争；于是旺季几乎夜夜客满。

若说天下有什么事最惹大巴比伦恼火——打个比方，最让它把背拱起来——那便是被人拿去同美国饭店相提并论，甚至混为一谈。大巴比伦坚决抵制美国式的吃、住、喝——尤其是美国式的喝。这样一想，朱尔被要求给西奥多·拉克索尔先生调一杯天使之吻时的那腔怨愤，便可想而知了。

“西奥多·拉克索尔先生可有同行的人？”朱尔接着同斯宾塞小姐说话。客人名字的每一个音节，他都不失分寸地缀上讥诮的重音。

“拉克索尔小姐——她住 111 号房。”

===Original===
Jules paused, and stroked his left whisker as it lay on his gleaming white collar.

“She’s where?” he queried, with a peculiar emphasis.

“No. 111. I couldn’t help it. There was no other room with a bathroom and dressing-room on that floor.” Miss Spencer’s voice had an appealing tone of excuse.

“Why didn’t you tell Mr. Theodore Racksole and Miss Racksole that we were unable to accommodate them?”

“Because Babs was within hearing.”

===Chinese===
朱尔顿住了，捋着他左边那绺搭在雪亮白衬领上的鬓胡。

“她住哪儿？”他问道，着重音的地方颇为特别。

“111 号房。我实在没法子。那一层再没有第二间同时带浴室和更衣室的客房了。”斯宾塞小姐的嗓音里满是告饶求恕的意味。

“你为什么不告诉西奥多·拉克索尔先生和拉克索尔小姐，就说本店接待不了他们？”

“因为‘巴布斯’（Babs）听得见。”

===Original===
Only three people in the wide world ever dreamt of applying to Mr. Félix Babylon the playful but mean abbreviation—Babs: those three were Jules, Miss Spencer, and Rocco. Jules had invented it. No one but he would have had either the wit or the audacity to do so.

“You’d better see that Miss Racksole changes her room tonight,” Jules said after another pause. “Leave it to me: I’ll fix it. Au revoir! It’s three minutes to eight. I shall take charge of the dining-room myself tonight.”

And Jules departed, rubbing his fine white hands slowly and meditatively. It was a trick of his, to rub his hands with a strange, roundabout motion, and the action denoted that some unusual excitement was in the air.

At eight o’clock precisely dinner was served in the immense salle-à-manger, that chaste yet splendid apartment of white and gold. At a small table near one of the windows a young lady sat alone. Her frocks said Paris, but her face unmistakably said New York. It was a self-possessed and bewitching face, the face of a woman thoroughly accustomed to doing exactly what she liked, when she liked, how she liked: the face of a woman who had taught hundreds of gilded young men the true art of fetching and carrying, and who, by twenty years or so of parental spoiling, had come to regard herself as the feminine equivalent of the Tsar of All the Russias. Such women are only made in America, and they only come to their full bloom in Europe, which they imagine to be a continent created by Providence for their diversion.

===Chinese===
普天之下，敢把“巴布斯”这个促狭而又刻薄的简称安到费利克斯·巴比伦先生头上的，只有三个人：朱尔、斯宾塞小姐和罗科。这简称是朱尔的发明。换了旁人，谁也没有这份机灵，更没有这份胆量。

“你最好今晚就让拉克索尔小姐把房间换了，”朱尔又停了一停，说道，“这事交给我，我来摆平。Au revoir，回见！8 点差 3 分，今晚餐厅归我亲自坐镇。”

朱尔这才离去，一面慢条斯理、若有所思地搓着他那双好看的白手。搓手是他的惯技：手势绕着古怪的圈子来回摩挲；这个动作一出现，便说明空气里起了某种不寻常的兴奋。

8 点整，晚饭在那间宏阔的餐厅（salle-à-manger）里开席——一色白与金的厅堂，素净而又华美。靠窗的一张小桌旁，孤零零坐着一位年轻小姐。她的连衣裙出自巴黎，她的面孔却明明白白写着纽约。那是一张沉稳自如、媚态天成的脸，属于那种“想做什么便做什么、想何时做便何时做、想怎样做便怎样做”惯透了的女性：这张脸的主人，曾教会成百上千的富家子弟真正懂得献殷勤、跑腿张罗的门道；又经父母二十来年的娇纵，她早已俨然自居为女版的全俄罗斯沙皇。这样的女人只有美国制造得出来，而她们唯有到了欧洲才开到全盛——欧洲么，她们认定是上苍特意造出来供自己消遣的一片大陆。

===Original===
The young lady by the window glanced disapprovingly at the menu card. Then she looked round the dining-room, and, while admiring the diners, decided that the room itself was rather small and plain. Then she gazed through the open window, and told herself that though the Thames by twilight was passable enough, it was by no means level with the Hudson, on whose shores her father had a hundred thousand dollar country cottage. Then she returned to the menu, and with a pursing of lovely lips said that there appeared to be nothing to eat.

“Sorry to keep you waiting, Nella.” It was Mr. Racksole, the intrepid millionaire who had dared to order an Angel Kiss in the smoke-room of the Grand Babylon. Nella—her proper name was Helen—smiled at her parent cautiously, reserving to herself the right to scold if she should feel so inclined.

“You always are late, father,” she said.

===Chinese===
窗边的年轻小姐不以为然地扫了一眼菜单。接着她环顾餐厅，在称许满堂宾客之余，判定这厅堂本身未免小了些、素了些。然后她望向敞开的窗外，暗自忖量：暮色里的泰晤士河尚可入目，却断然比不得哈德逊河——她父亲在哈德逊河畔置有一幢十万美元的乡间别墅。末了她又回到菜单上，可爱的双唇一撅，断言看上去竟没有一样东西可吃。

“抱歉，内拉（Nella），叫你久等了。”说话的是拉克索尔先生——那位胆敢在大巴比伦吸烟室点叫天使之吻的无畏百万富翁。内拉——她的正式名字是海伦（Helen）——朝父亲矜持地一笑，把数落的权利留在手里，觉得该用时随时可用。

“你总是迟到，爸爸。”她说。

===Original===
“Only on a holiday,” he added. “What is there to eat?”

“Nothing.”

“Then let’s have it. I’m hungry. I’m never so hungry as when I’m being seriously idle.”

“*Consommé Britannia*,” she began to read out from the menu, “*Saumon d’Ecosse*, *Sauce Genoise*, *Aspics de Homard*. Oh, heavens! Who wants these horrid messes on a night like this?”

“But, Nella, this is the best cooking in Europe,” he protested.

===Chinese===
“只有度假的时候才这样。”他补了一句。“有什么可吃的？”

“没有。”

“那就要吧。我饿了。人一认真闲下来，我从没饿得这么厉害过。”

“*Consommé Britannia*（英式清炖牛肉汤），”她开始照着菜单往外念，“*Saumon d’Ecosse*（苏格兰煨鲑鱼）、*Sauce Genoise*（热那亚沙司）、*Aspics de Homard*（龙虾冻）。哦，天哪！这样的夜晚，谁要吃这些乌七八糟的浆糊？”

“可是，内拉，这可是全欧洲最上等的厨艺。”他抗辩道。

===Original===
“Say, father,” she said, with seeming irrelevance, “had you forgotten it’s my birthday tomorrow?”

“Have I ever forgotten your birthday, O most costly daughter?”

“On the whole you’ve been a most satisfactory dad,” she answered sweetly, “and to reward you I’ll be content this year with the cheapest birthday treat you ever gave me. Only I’ll have it tonight.”

“Well,” he said, with the long-suffering patience, the readiness for any surprise, of a parent whom Nella had thoroughly trained, “what is it?”

“It’s this. Let’s have filleted steak and a bottle of Bass for dinner tonight. It will be simply exquisite. I shall love it.”

===Chinese===
“我说，爸爸，”她没头没脑地起了这么个话头，“你是不是忘了，明天是我的生日？”

“我几时忘过你的生日，哦，我代价最昂贵的女儿？”

“总的说来，你一直是个最令人满意的爸爸，”她甜甜地回敬，“为了犒劳你，今年我就心满意足地领受你给过我的最便宜的一回生日待遇。只不过，这一回我要放在今晚。”

“哦，”他说，带着那种久经磨难的耐心、那种随时预备迎接意外的从容——这是被内拉调教得服服帖帖的父亲才有的修为，“你想要什么？”

“是这么回事。今晚咱们晚饭就吃菲力牛排，配一瓶巴斯啤酒（Bass）。那才叫妙不可言。我准会爱死的。”

===Original===
“But my dear Nella,” he exclaimed, “steak and beer at Félix’s! It’s impossible! Moreover, young women still under twenty-three cannot be permitted to drink Bass.”

“I said steak and Bass, and as for being twenty-three, shall be going in twenty-four tomorrow.”

Miss Racksole set her small white teeth.

There was a gentle cough. Jules stood over them. It must have been out of a pure spirit of adventure that he had selected this table for his own services. Usually Jules did not personally wait at dinner. He merely hovered observant, like a captain on the bridge during the mate’s watch. Regular frequenters of the hotel felt themselves honoured when Jules attached himself to their tables.

===Chinese===
“可是我亲爱的内拉，”他嚷了起来，“在费利克斯家吃牛排喝啤酒！这不可能！再说，还没满 23 岁的年轻女子，不许喝巴斯啤酒。”

“我说的是菲力牛排和巴斯啤酒。至于 23 岁——明天我就奔 24 去了。”

拉克索尔小姐咬紧了她那口小巧的白牙。

一声斯文的咳嗽。朱尔已经站在他们桌旁。他挑中这张桌子亲自服侍，想必纯粹出于冒险的兴致。往常朱尔并不亲自伺候晚饭；他只在高处盘桓、眼观六路，好比大副值班时舰桥上的那位船长。本店的常客，若得朱尔亲自照应本桌，无不自觉身价倍增。

===Original===
Theodore Racksole hesitated one second, and then issued the order with a fine air of carelessness:

“Filleted steak for two, and a bottle of Bass.” It was the bravest act of Theodore Racksole’s life, and yet at more than one previous crisis a high courage had not been lacking to him.

“It’s not in the menu, sir,” said Jules the imperturbable.

“Never mind. Get it. We want it.”

“Very good, sir.”

===Chinese===
西奥多·拉克索尔迟疑了一秒钟，随即摆出一副潇洒的满不在乎，下达了点菜的吩咐：

“两份菲力牛排，外加一瓶巴斯啤酒。”这是西奥多·拉克索尔一生中最勇敢的壮举——尽管在此前不止一次的危急关头，他从来不缺过人的胆气。

“菜单上没有这个，先生。”岿然不动的朱尔说。

“不要紧。去取来。我们要这个。”

“遵命，先生。”

===Original===
Jules walked to the service-door, and, merely affecting to look behind, came immediately back again.

“Mr. Rocco’s compliments, sir, and he regrets to be unable to serve steak and Bass tonight, sir.”

“Mr. Rocco?” questioned Racksole lightly.

“Mr. Rocco,” repeated Jules with firmness.

“And who is Mr. Rocco?”

===Chinese===
朱尔走向备餐间的门，只装模作样地朝里面望了一眼，便立刻折返回来。

“罗科先生向先生致意，先生；他深为遗憾，今晚无法供应牛排和巴斯啤酒，先生。”

“罗科先生？”拉克索尔轻描淡写地问。

“罗科先生。”朱尔一字一顿地重申。

“那么，罗科先生又是何许人？”

===Original===
“Mr. Rocco is our chef, sir.” Jules had the expression of a man who is asked to explain who Shakespeare was.

The two men looked at each other. It seemed incredible that Theodore Racksole, the ineffable Racksole, who owned a thousand miles of railway, several towns, and sixty votes in Congress, should be defied by a waiter, or even by a whole hotel. Yet so it was. When Europe’s effete back is against the wall not a regiment of millionaires can turn its flank. Jules had the calm expression of a strong man sure of victory. His face said: “You beat me once, but not this time, my New York friend!”

As for Nella, knowing her father, she foresaw interesting events, and waited confidently for the steak. She did not feel hungry, and she could afford to wait.

“Excuse me a moment, Nella,” said Theodore Racksole quietly, “I shall be back in about two seconds,” and he strode out of the salle-à-manger. No one in the room recognized the millionaire, for he was unknown to London, this being his first visit to Europe for over twenty years. Had anyone done so, and caught the expression on his face, that man might have trembled for an explosion which should have blown the entire Grand Babylon into the Thames.

Jules retired strategically to a corner. He had fired; it was the antagonist’s turn. A long and varied experience had taught Jules that a guest who embarks on the subjugation of a waiter is almost always lost; the waiter has so many advantages in such a contest.

===Chinese===
“罗科先生是我们的主厨，先生。”朱尔脸上的神情，活像有人请他解释莎士比亚是谁。

两个人彼此对望。西奥多·拉克索尔——无可比拟的拉克索尔——名下有一千英里铁路、好几座城镇、国会里 60 张选票，此刻竟遭一个侍者、甚至一整座饭店公然顶撞，这似乎难以置信。然而事情就是如此。当欧洲那副疲朽的身板背抵着墙时，纵有一个团的百万富翁，也迂回不了它的侧翼。朱尔带着强者稳操胜券的安然神色。他的脸在说：“你赢过我一回，可这一回不行，我的纽约朋友！”

至于内拉，她深知自己的父亲，料定有趣的变故就在眼前，便笃笃定定地等她的牛排。她并不觉得饿，她等得起。

“失陪片刻，内拉，”西奥多·拉克索尔平静地说，“我约莫两秒钟就回来。”说罢大步走出了餐厅。满厅没有人认出这位百万富翁，伦敦无人识得他——这是他二十多年来头一遭踏上欧洲。设若有谁认出了他，又恰巧看清他脸上的神色，那人多半要为一场行将到来的爆炸两腿发软——那爆炸足以把整座大巴比伦掀进泰晤士河。

朱尔看准形势，退到一个角落里。他已经开火；这会儿轮到对手。长期而繁复的经验教会朱尔：凡是存心要降服一个侍者的客人，几乎总要吃败仗；在这样的较量里，侍者手里的便宜实在太多了。

## II How Mr. Racksole Obtained His Dinner / II 拉克索尔先生如何吃上晚餐

===Original===

Nevertheless, there are men with a confirmed habit of getting their own way, even as guests in an exclusive hotel: and Theodore Racksole had long since fallen into that useful practice﻿—except when his only daughter Helen, motherless but high-spirited girl, chose to think that his way crossed hers, in which case Theodore capitulated and fell back. But when Theodore and his daughter happened to be going one and the same road, which was pretty often, then Heaven alone might help any obstacle that was so ill-advised as to stand in their path. Jules, great and observant man though he was, had not noticed the terrible projecting chins of both father and daughter, otherwise it is possible he would have reconsidered the question of the steak and Bass.

Theodore Racksole went direct to the entrance-hall of the hotel, and entered Miss Spencer’s sanctum.

“I want to see Mr. Babylon,” he said, “without the delay of an instant.”

Miss Spencer leisurely raised her flaxen head.

===Chinese===

不过，世上有一种人，素来养成了事事由自己做主的习惯，哪怕在一家门槛极高的饭店里做客人也不例外；西奥多·拉克索尔（Theodore Racksole）早已把这门受用的功夫练得炉火纯青——唯独他的独生女海伦（Helen）例外。海伦是个幼年丧母、却意气飞扬的姑娘，一旦她认定父亲的路数与自己的路数撞了车，西奥多必定缴械后撤。可每当父女俩恰好同走一条路——这种时候其实不少——那么，哪个不长眼的东西胆敢挡在道上，就只有老天爷才救得了它。朱尔（Jules）虽是个人精，眼观六路，却没留意到这对父女那两副威风凛凛、向前突出的下巴；要不然，他说不定会重新掂量一下那份牛排和那瓶巴斯（Bass）啤酒的买卖。

西奥多·拉克索尔径直走向饭店门厅，走进了斯宾塞小姐（Miss Spencer）的私室。

“我要见巴比伦先生，”他说，“一分钟也不能耽搁。”

斯宾塞小姐慢悠悠地抬起她那颗亚麻色的头颅。

===Original===

“I am afraid﻿—” she began the usual formula. It was part of her daily duty to discourage guests who desired to see Mr. Babylon.

“No, no,” said Racksole quickly, “I don’t want any ‘I’m afraids.’ This is business. If you had been the ordinary hotel clerk I should have slipped you a couple of sovereigns into your hand, and the thing would have been done. As you are not﻿—as you are obviously above bribes﻿—I merely say to you, I must see Mr. Babylon at once on an affair of the utmost urgency. My name is Racksole﻿—Theodore Racksole.”

“Of New York?” questioned a voice at the door, with a slight foreign accent.

The millionaire turned sharply, and saw a rather short, French-looking man, with a bald head, a grey beard, a long and perfectly-built frock coat, eyeglasses attached to a minute silver chain, and blue eyes that seemed to have the transparent innocence of a maid’s.

===Chinese===

“恐怕——”她开始搬出那套照例的说辞。劝阻想见巴比伦先生的客人，本是她日常分内之事。

“不，不，”拉克索尔抢着说，“我不要听什么‘恐怕’。这是正经生意。你若是个寻常的饭店文书，我早就在你手心里塞上两枚金镑（sovereign），事情也就办妥了。你既然不是——你显然是贿赂不动的——那我只跟你说一句：我有十万火急的事，必须立刻见到巴比伦先生。我姓拉克索尔——西奥多·拉克索尔。”

“纽约的那位？”门口一个声音问道，带着轻微的外国口音。

百万富翁猛地转过身，只见一个身材偏矮、一脸法国相的男人：秃顶，灰白胡须，一件剪裁得无可挑剔的长礼服，夹鼻眼镜上系着一根极细的银链，一双蓝眼睛，透着少女一般透明无邪的天真。

===Original===

“There is only one,” said Theodore Racksole succinctly.

“You wish to see me?” the newcomer suggested.

“You are Mr. Félix Babylon?”

The man bowed.

“At this moment I wish to see you more than anyone else in the world,” said Racksole. “I am consumed and burnt up with a desire to see you, Mr. Babylon. I only want a few minutes’ quiet chat. I fancy I can settle my business in that time.”

===Chinese===

“天下只有一个。”西奥多·拉克索尔言简意赅。

“您想见的是我？”来人试探道。

“您就是费利克斯·巴比伦（Félix Babylon）先生？”

那人躬了躬身。

“此时此刻，这世上我最想见的人就是您。”拉克索尔说，“想见您的念头把我烧得五内如焚，巴比伦先生。我只求几分钟安安静静的交谈。我估摸着，这点工夫够我把正事谈妥。”

===Original===

With a gesture Mr. Babylon invited the millionaire down a side corridor, at the end of which was Mr. Babylon’s private room, a miracle of Louis XV furniture and tapestry: like most unmarried men with large incomes, Mr. Babylon had “tastes” of a highly expensive sort.

The landlord and his guest sat down opposite each other. Theodore Racksole had met with the usual millionaire’s luck in this adventure, for Mr. Babylon made a practice of not allowing himself to be interviewed by his guests, however distinguished, however wealthy, however pertinacious. If he had not chanced to enter Miss Spencer’s office at that precise moment, and if he had not been impressed in a somewhat peculiar way by the physiognomy of the millionaire, not all Mr. Racksole’s American energy and ingenuity would have availed for a confabulation with the owner of the Grand Babylon Hotel that night. Theodore Racksole, however, was ignorant that a mere accident had served him. He took all the credit to himself.

“I read in the New York papers some months ago,” Theodore started, without even a clearing of the throat, “that this hotel of yours, Mr. Babylon, was to be sold to a limited company, but it appears that the sale was not carried out.”

===Chinese===

巴比伦先生做了个手势，请这位百万富翁沿一条侧廊走去，廊底便是巴比伦先生的私人房间——满屋的路易十五式（Louis XV）家具与织锦挂毯，俨然一桩奇迹：和大多数进项丰厚的单身汉一样，巴比伦先生的“品味”也属于极其昂贵的那一类。

饭店主人与客人面对面坐下。这桩事里，西奥多·拉克索尔照例交上了百万富翁的好运，因为巴比伦先生素来有个规矩：任凭客人如何显贵、如何豪富、如何缠磨，一概不接受面谈。倘若他不是恰好在那个节骨眼上走进斯宾塞小姐的办公室，倘若这位百万富翁的面相没有以某种颇为特别的方式给他留下印象，那么，凭拉克索尔先生满身的美国劲头和机巧，当晚也休想与大巴比伦饭店（the Grand Babylon Hotel）的东家对坐一谈。不过，西奥多·拉克索尔并不知道自己是沾了一桩偶然的光。功劳，他全记在了自己账上。

“几个月前，我在纽约的报纸上读到，”西奥多开了腔，连清嗓子的过场都省了，“说是您这家饭店，巴比伦先生，要出让给一家股份有限公司，可看样子这笔买卖并没做成。”

===Original===

“It was not,” answered Mr. Babylon frankly, “and the reason was that the middlemen between the proposed company and myself wished to make a large secret profit, and I declined to be a party to such a profit. They were firm; I was firm; and so the affair came to nothing.”

“The agreed price was satisfactory?”

“Quite.”

“May I ask what the price was?”

“Are you a buyer, Mr. Racksole?”

“Are you a seller, Mr. Babylon?”

===Chinese===

“是没有做成。”巴比伦先生坦然相答，“缘由在于：拟议中的公司与我之间的那批中间人，想从中赚一笔数目可观的暗盘利润，我不肯参与这种勾当。他们不让步，我也不让步，事情便这样告吹了。”

“谈定的价钱还令人满意？”

“十分满意。”

“可否请教，价钱是多少？”

“您是买主吗，拉克索尔先生？”

“您是卖主吗，巴比伦先生？”

===Original===

“I am,” said Babylon, “on terms. The price was four hundred thousand pounds, including the leasehold and goodwill. But I sell only on the condition that the buyer does not transfer the property to a limited company at a higher figure.”

“I will put one question to you, Mr. Babylon,” said the millionaire. “What have your profits averaged during the last four years?”

“Thirty-four thousand pounds per annum.”

“I buy,” said Theodore Racksole, smiling contentedly; “and we will, if you please, exchange contract-letters on the spot.”

===Chinese===

“是卖主。”巴比伦说，“但讲条件。价钱是四十万英镑，租赁权连同商誉一并算在内。只是我卖，附带一个条件：买主不得把产业加价转卖给一家股份有限公司。”

“我只向您提一个问题，巴比伦先生。”百万富翁说，“最近四年，您的利润平均几何？”

“每年三万四千英镑。”

“我买了。”西奥多·拉克索尔心满意足地笑道，“若您不弃，咱们当场就交换契据函件。”

===Original===

“You come quickly to a resolution, Mr. Racksole. But perhaps you have been considering this question for a long time?”

“On the contrary,” Racksole looked at his watch, “I have been considering it for six minutes.”

Félix Babylon bowed, as one thoroughly accustomed to eccentricity of wealth.

“The beauty of being well-known,” Racksole continued, “is that you needn’t trouble about preliminary explanations. You, Mr. Babylon, probably know all about me. I know a good deal about you. We can take each other for granted without reference. Really, it is as simple to buy an hotel or a railroad as it is to buy a watch, provided one is equal to the transaction.”

“Precisely,” agreed Mr. Babylon smiling. “Shall we draw up the little informal contract? There are details to be thought of. But it occurs to me that you cannot have dined yet, and might prefer to deal with minor questions after dinner.”

===Chinese===

“您决断得好快，拉克索尔先生。不过，此事想必您已思量多时了吧？”

“恰恰相反。”拉克索尔看了看表，“我思量了六分钟。”

费利克斯·巴比伦躬了躬身，一副对豪富的乖僻早已司空见惯的气度。

“出了名的妙处，”拉克索尔接着说，“就在于无须费神铺垫解释。巴比伦先生，我的底细您大概一清二楚；您的事我也知道得不少。咱们用不着彼此查证，径直把对方当作信得过的现成人选。说真的，只要自家撑得起这场交易，买一家饭店、买一条铁路，跟买一块怀表一样简单。”

“正是。”巴比伦先生含笑赞同，“我们就把这份非正式的小契约拟起来，如何？还有些细节要斟酌。不过我想到，您还没有用过晚餐，或许愿意把次要的问题留到饭后再去料理。”

===Original===

“I have not dined,” said the millionaire, with emphasis, “and in that connection will you do me a favour? Will you send for Mr. Rocco?”

“You wish to see him, naturally.”

“I do,” said the millionaire, and added, “about my dinner.”

“Rocco is a great man,” murmured Mr. Babylon as he touched the bell, ignoring the last words. “My compliments to Mr. Rocco,” he said to the page who answered his summons, “and if it is quite convenient I should be glad to see him here for a moment.”

“What do you give Rocco?” Racksole inquired.

===Chinese===

“还没吃。”百万富翁加重语气说，“既然说到吃，您肯帮个忙吗？派人把罗科先生请来。”

“您要见他，这是自然。”

“正是。”百万富翁答了，又补上一句，“为我的晚餐。”

“罗科是位了不起的人物。”巴比伦先生按铃时低声说道，对那后半句只作未闻。他吩咐应召而来的小侍者：“替我向罗科先生致意，倘若那边方便，请他到这里来稍坐片刻。”

“您给罗科开多少？”拉克索尔问。

===Original===

“Two thousand a year and the treatment of an ambassador.”

“I shall give him the treatment of an ambassador and three thousand.”

“You will be wise,” said Félix Babylon.

At that moment Rocco came into the room, very softly﻿—a man of forty, thin, with long, thin hands, and an inordinately long brown silky moustache.

“Rocco,” said Félix Babylon, “let me introduce Mr. Theodore Racksole, of New York.”

===Chinese===

“一年两千，外加大使级的礼遇。”

“我给他大使级的礼遇，外加三千。”

“您是明智的。”费利克斯·巴比伦说。

恰在此刻，罗科悄没声儿地进了屋——四十上下，清瘦，一双手瘦而长，蓄着一把长得离谱的棕色丝光髭须。

“罗科，”费利克斯·巴比伦道，“容我引见：这位是纽约来的西奥多·拉克索尔先生。”

===Original===

“Sharmed,” said Rocco, bowing. “Ze﻿—ze, vat you call it, millionaire?”

“Exactly,” Racksole put in, and continued quickly: “Mr. Rocco, I wish to acquaint you before any other person with the fact that I have purchased the Grand Babylon Hotel. If you think well to afford me the privilege of retaining your services I shall be happy to offer you a remuneration of three thousand a year.”

“Tree, you said?”

“Three.”

“Sharmed.”

“And now, Mr. Rocco, will you oblige me very much by ordering a plain beefsteak and a bottle of Bass to be served by Jules﻿—I particularly desire Jules﻿—at table No. 17 in the dining-room in ten minutes from now? And will you do me the honour of lunching with me tomorrow?”

===Chinese===

“幸会（Sharmed）。”罗科鞠着躬说，“这——这位，你们怎么称呼来着，百万富翁？”

“没错。”拉克索尔插进话头，紧接着快人快语：“罗科先生，我要让你赶在任何人之前知道一件事：大巴比伦饭店已经归了我。你若肯赏脸让我继续聘用，我乐意一年出三千英镑。”

“三千，你说的是？”

“三千。”

“幸会。”

“那么现在，罗科先生，劳你大驾安排一份家常牛排、一瓶巴斯啤酒，十分钟之后送到餐厅 17 号桌，由朱尔端上来——我特别指名要朱尔。另外，明天中午肯不肯赏光，跟我一起吃顿午饭？”

===Original===

Mr. Rocco gasped, bowed, muttered something in French, and departed.

Five minutes later the buyer and seller of the Grand Babylon Hotel had each signed a curt document, scribbled out on the hotel notepaper. Félix Babylon asked no questions, and it was this heroic absence of curiosity, of surprise on his part, that more than anything else impressed Theodore Racksole. How many hotel proprietors in the world, Racksole asked himself, would have let that beefsteak and Bass go by without a word of comment.

“From what date do you wish the purchase to take effect?” asked Babylon.

“Oh,” said Racksole lightly, “it doesn’t matter. Shall we say from tonight?”

===Chinese===

罗科先生倒抽一口气，深深一躬，用法语咕哝了句什么，退了出去。

五分钟后，大巴比伦饭店的买主与卖主各自在一份寥寥数行、草草写在饭店信笺上的文书上签了字。费利克斯·巴比伦一个问题也没有问；正是他这份英雄气概式的不好奇、不吃惊，比什么都更让西奥多·拉克索尔心里叹服。普天之下的饭店老板，拉克索尔扪心自问，有几个能眼看着那份牛排和那瓶巴斯从眼皮底下过去，而不置一词？

“这笔买卖，您想让它从哪天生效？”巴比伦问。

“哦，”拉克索尔轻描淡写，“无所谓。就说从今晚起，如何？”

===Original===

“As you will. I have long wished to retire. And now that the moment has come﻿—and so dramatically﻿—I am ready. I shall return to Switzerland. One cannot spend much money there, but it is my native land. I shall be the richest man in Switzerland.” He smiled with a kind of sad amusement.

“I suppose you are fairly well off?” said Racksole, in that easy familiar style of his, as though the idea had just occurred to him.

“Besides what I shall receive from you, I have half a million invested.”

“Then you will be nearly a millionaire?”

Félix Babylon nodded.

===Chinese===

“悉听尊便。我久已想退隐了。如今这一刻终于到来——而且来得这样富于戏剧性——我已准备停当。我要回瑞士去。在那儿钱没处多花，可那是我的故土。我将成为瑞士最富有的人。”他微微一笑，笑意里含着几分悲凉的余味。

“想必您家底也还殷实？”拉克索尔用他那套随和熟络的腔调说，仿佛这念头方才冒出来。

“除了将从您这里收进的那笔，我还有五十万英镑在外面放着生息。”

“这么着，您就差不多是位百万富翁了？”

费利克斯·巴比伦点了点头。

===Original===

“I congratulate you, my dear sir,” said Racksole, in the tone of a judge addressing a newly-admitted barrister. “Nine hundred thousand pounds, expressed in francs, will sound very nice﻿—in Switzerland.”

“Of course to you, Mr. Racksole, such a sum would be poverty. Now if one might guess at your own wealth?” Félix Babylon was imitating the other’s freedom.

“I do not know, to five millions or so, what I am worth,” said Racksole, with sincerity, his tone indicating that he would have been glad to give the information if it were in his power.

“You have had anxieties, Mr. Racksole?”

“Still have them. I am now holiday-making in London with my daughter in order to get rid of them for a time.”

===Chinese===

“恭喜您，我亲爱的先生。”拉克索尔说，那口气活像法官向一名新近取得出庭资格的律师道贺，“九十万英镑，折成法郎，听着会很受用——在瑞士。”

“当然，对您来说，拉克索尔先生，这样一笔钱无异于清贫。那么，可容我斗胆猜一猜您自家的身家？”费利克斯·巴比伦也学起了对方那份放言无忌。

“我自己也说不准——五百万上下吧。”拉克索尔答得诚恳，那语气分明表示：但凡他能报个准数，他倒很乐意奉告。

“您也有烦心的事，拉克索尔先生？”

“正烦着呢。眼下我带着女儿在伦敦度假，就是为了把那些事撂开一阵子。”

===Original===

“Is the purchase of hotels your notion of relaxation, then?”

Racksole shrugged his shoulders. “It is a change from railroads,” he laughed.

“Ah, my friend, you little know what you have bought.”

“Oh! yes I do,” returned Racksole; “I have bought just the first hotel in the world.”

“That is true, that is true,” Babylon admitted, gazing meditatively at the antique Persian carpet. “There is nothing, anywhere, like my hotel. But you will regret the purchase, Mr. Racksole. It is no business of mine, of course, but I cannot help repeating that you will regret the purchase.”

===Chinese===

“这么说，买饭店便是您休憩身心的法子？”

拉克索尔耸了耸肩。“算是从铁路上换换花样。”他笑道。

“唉，我的朋友，您还不知道自己买下的是一样什么东西。”

“哦！我恰恰知道。”拉克索尔回敬道，“我买下的，正是天下第一的饭店。”

“这话说得对，这话说得对。”巴比伦承认道，若有所思地凝视着那块古董波斯地毯，“天南地北，再没有能与我这家饭店相提并论的了。可您会为这笔买卖后悔的，拉克索尔先生。这自然碍不着我什么事，可我忍不住要再说一遍：您会为这笔买卖后悔的。”

===Original===

“I never regret.”

“Then you will begin very soon﻿—perhaps tonight.”

“Why do you say that?”

“Because the Grand Babylon is the Grand Babylon. You think because you control a railroad, or an ironworks, or a line of steamers, therefore you can control anything. But no. Not the Grand Babylon. There is something about the Grand Babylon﻿—” He threw up his hands.

“Servants rob you, of course.”

===Chinese===

“我从不后悔。”

“那您很快就要开了这个头——说不定就在今晚。”

“您为什么这么说？”

“因为大巴比伦就是大巴比伦。您以为，既然管得住一条铁路、一座钢铁厂、一家轮船公司，便什么都管得住。不。管不住大巴比伦。这地方有种东西——”他两手一摊。

“仆役会偷您的东西，自然有。”

===Original===

“Of course. I suppose I lose a hundred pounds a week in that way. But it is not that I mean. It is the guests. The guests are too﻿—too distinguished. The great ambassadors, the great financiers, the great nobles, all the men that move the world, put up under my roof. London is the centre of everything, and my hotel﻿—your hotel﻿—is the centre of London. Once I had a King and a Dowager Empress staying here at the same time. Imagine that!”

“A great honour, Mr. Babylon. But wherein lies the difficulty?”

“Mr. Racksole,” was the grim reply, “what has become of your shrewdness﻿—that shrewdness which has made your fortune so immense that even you cannot calculate it? Do you not perceive that the roof which habitually shelters all the force, all the authority of the world, must necessarily also shelter nameless and numberless plotters, schemers, evildoers, and workers of mischief? The thing is as clear as day﻿—and as dark as night. Mr. Racksole, I never know by whom I am surrounded. I never know what is going forward. Only sometimes I get hints, glimpses of strange acts and strange secrets. You mentioned my servants. They are almost all good servants, skilled, competent. But what are they besides? For anything I know my fourth sub-chef may be an agent of some European government. For anything I know my invaluable Miss Spencer may be in the pay of a court dressmaker or a Frankfort banker. Even Rocco may be someone else in addition to Rocco.”

“That makes it all the more interesting,” remarked Theodore Racksole.

===Chinese===

“自然有。这样算来，一星期怕要折损一百镑。可我指的不是这个，是客人。我们的客人太——太显赫了。各国大使、金融巨擘、豪门贵胄，一切搅动世界的角色，都在我这个屋檐下落脚。伦敦是一切的中心，而我的饭店——您的饭店——正是伦敦的中心。有一回，一位国王和一位皇太后同时住在我这里。您想想看！”

“那是天大的荣耀，巴比伦先生。可难处在哪儿呢？”

“拉克索尔先生，”对方冷冷答道，“您那副精明到哪儿去了？就是那副精明，替您挣下了连您自己都算不清的泼大家业。难道您看不出：这个屋顶既惯常庇护着全世界所有的势力、所有的威仪，就必然也庇护着数不清的无名密谋家、策士、作恶之徒与生事的行家？这道理明如白昼——又暗如长夜。拉克索尔先生，我从来不知道围在我身边的是些什么人，从来不知道正在进行着什么。只是偶尔，我会得些风声，瞥见些怪事与隐秘的一角。您提起了我的仆役。他们几乎个个是好仆役，娴熟，称职。可除此之外，他们还是什么人？天晓得，我的第四副厨说不定是欧洲某国政府的坐探；我那位无价之宝的斯宾塞小姐，说不定正领着某位宫廷裁缝或法兰克福银行家的薪水；就连罗科，也未必只是罗科。”

“那倒越发有意思了。”西奥多·拉克索尔说。

---

===Original===

“What a long time you have been, Father,” said Nella, when he returned to table No. 17 in the salle-à-manger.

“Only twenty minutes, my dove.”

“But you said two seconds. There is a difference.”

“Well, you see, I had to wait for the steak to cook.”

“Did you have much trouble in getting my birthday treat?”

===Chinese===

“你可去了好半天哪，爸爸。”他回到餐厅（salle-à-manger）的 17 号桌时，内拉（Nella）说。

“才二十分钟，我的小鸽子。”

“可你说的是两秒钟。这可有出入。”

“这个嘛，你瞧，总得等牛排烧熟。”

“给我张罗这份生日大餐，费了不少周折吧？”

===Original===

“No trouble. But it didn’t come quite as cheap as you said.”

“What do you mean, Father?”

“Only that I’ve bought the entire hotel. But don’t split.”

“Father, you always were a delicious parent. Shall you give me the hotel for a birthday present?”

“No. I shall run it﻿—as an amusement. By the way, who is that chair for?”

===Chinese===

“不费事。不过它来得可不像你说的那么便宜。”

“您这话是什么意思，爸爸？”

“没什么，意思是整座饭店都归了我了。不过，先别声张。”

“爸爸，你一向是位‘可口’的家长。那您是打算把饭店当生日礼物送给我咯？”

“不。我来经营它——权当解闷。对了，那把椅子是给谁留的？”

===Original===

He noticed that a third cover had been laid at the table.

“That is for a friend of mine who came in about five minutes ago. Of course I told him he must share our steak. He’ll be here in a moment.”

“May I respectfully inquire his name?”

“Dimmock﻿—Christian name Reginald; profession, English companion to Prince Aribert of Posen. I met him when I was in St. Petersburg with cousin Hetty last fall. Oh; here he is. Mr. Dimmock, this is my dear father. He has succeeded with the steak.”

Theodore Racksole found himself confronted by a very young man, with deep black eyes, and a fresh, boyish expression. They began to talk.

===Chinese===

他注意到桌上已经摆下第三份餐具。

“那是给我的一位朋友的，他约莫五分钟前进来的。我自然跟他说了，他得跟咱们合吃这份牛排。他马上就到。”

“容我恭恭敬敬地问一句，他贵姓？”

“迪莫克（Dimmock）——教名雷金纳德（Reginald）；职业，波森的阿里贝尔特王子（Prince Aribert of Posen）的英国侍从官。去年秋天我陪表妹海蒂（Hetty）去圣彼得堡时认识了他。哦，他来了。迪莫克先生，这是我亲爱的父亲。他把牛排的事办成了。”

西奥多·拉克索尔发觉自己面对的是一个十分年轻的男子，黑眼睛深湛，一脸鲜嫩的孩子气。两人攀谈起来。

===Original===

Jules approached with the steak. Racksole tried to catch the waiter’s eye, but could not. The dinner proceeded.

“Oh, Father!” cried Nella, “what a lot of mustard you have taken!”

“Have I?” he said, and then he happened to glance into a mirror on his left hand between two windows. He saw the reflection of Jules, who stood behind his chair, and he saw Jules give a slow, significant, ominous wink to Mr. Dimmock﻿—Christian name, Reginald.

He examined his mustard in silence. He thought that perhaps he had helped himself rather plenteously to mustard.

===Chinese===

朱尔端着牛排走上前来。拉克索尔想跟这位领班递个眼色，却没能如愿。晚餐照常进行。

“哎呀，爸爸！”内拉叫了起来，“你可搁了好多芥末！”

“是吗？”他说着，恰好瞟见左手边两扇窗之间的一面镜子。他看见朱尔的映影——朱尔就立在他的椅背后——又看见朱尔朝迪莫克先生——教名，雷金纳德——慢悠悠地、意味深长地、不怀好意地眨了一下眼。

他默默端详着自己那份芥末。他想，自己兴许当真下手重了些，多舀了些芥末。

## III At Three a.m. / III 凌晨三点

===Original===
Mr. Reginald Dimmock proved himself, despite his extreme youth, to be a man of the world and of experiences, and a practised talker. Conversation between him and Nella Racksole seemed never to flag. They chattered about St. Petersburg, and the ice on the Neva, and the tenor at the opera who had been exiled to Siberia, and the quality of Russian tea, and the sweetness of Russian champagne, and various other aspects of Muscovite existence. Russia exhausted, Nella lightly outlined her own doings since she had met the young man in the Tsar’s capital, and this recital brought the topic round to London, where it stayed till the final piece of steak was eaten. Theodore Racksole noticed that Mr. Dimmock gave very meagre information about his own movements, either past or future. He regarded the youth as a typical hanger-on of Courts, and wondered how he had obtained his post of companion to Prince Aribert of Posen, and who Prince Aribert of Posen might be. The millionaire thought he had once heard of Posen, but he wasn’t sure; he rather fancied it was one of those small nondescript German States of which five-sixths of the subjects are Palace officials, and the rest charcoal-burners or innkeepers. Until the meal was nearly over, Racksole said little—perhaps his thoughts were too busy with Jules’ wink to Mr. Dimmock, but when ices had been followed by coffee, he decided that it might be as well, in the interests of the hotel, to discover something about his daughter’s friend. He never for an instant questioned her right to possess her own friends; he had always left her in the most amazing liberty, relying on her inherited good sense to keep her out of mischief; but, quite apart from the wink, he was struck by Nella’s attitude towards Mr. Dimmock, an attitude in which an amiable scorn was blended with an evident desire to propitiate and please.

===Chinese===
雷金纳德·迪莫克（Reginald Dimmock）先生虽然年纪极轻，却证明自己是个见惯世面、颇有阅历的人，而且是个老练的谈客。他与内拉·拉克索尔（Nella Racksole）之间的交谈似乎从无冷场。两人聊圣彼得堡，聊涅瓦河上的冰，聊歌剧院里那位被流放去西伯利亚的男高音，聊俄国茶的品质，聊俄国香槟的甜头，还聊了俄罗斯生活的种种其他侧面。俄国话题说尽，内拉轻描淡写地讲起自己自沙皇都城结识这位年轻人以来的种种行迹，这番叙述又把话题引到了伦敦，此后便一直停在伦敦，直到最后一块牛排吃完为止。西奥多·拉克索尔（Theodore Racksole）注意到，迪莫克先生无论对过去还是将来的自身行踪，都吐露得极少。他把这青年看作典型的宫廷帮闲，心里纳闷他是怎样谋得波森的阿里贝尔特王子（Prince Aribert of Posen）侍从官这个职位的，而这位波森的阿里贝尔特王子又究竟是何等人物。百万富翁觉得自己仿佛听说过波森，却拿不准；他倒觉得十有八九那是那种说不上名目的德意志小邦之一——六分之五的臣民在宫廷里当差，其余的不是烧炭匠就是小客栈老板。直到用餐将尽，拉克索尔一直少言寡语——也许他的心思全叫朱尔（Jules）抛给迪莫克先生的那个眼色占去了；不过，当冰点之后端上咖啡，他断定，为了饭店的利益，不妨摸一摸女儿这位朋友的底细。他从未有一刻质疑过女儿拥有自己朋友的权利；他向来放手给她大得惊人的自由，全凭她承袭来的那份见识分寸去免惹是非；可是，撇开那个眼色不谈，内拉对待迪莫克先生的态度也令他纳罕——那态度里，一种和善的轻蔑掺和着一种明显的曲意讨好。

===Original===
“Nella tells me, Mr. Dimmock, that you hold a confidential position with Prince Aribert of Posen,” said Racksole. “You will pardon an American’s ignorance, but is Prince Aribert a reigning Prince—what, I believe, you call in Europe, a Prince Regnant?”

“His Highness is not a reigning Prince, nor ever likely to be,” answered Dimmock. “The Grand Ducal Throne of Posen is occupied by his Highness’s nephew, the Grand Duke Eugen.”

“Nephew?” cried Nella with astonishment.

“Why not, dear lady?”

===Chinese===
“内拉告诉我，迪莫克先生，你在波森的阿里贝尔特王子手下担任机要之职，”拉克索尔说，“还请原谅一个美国人的无知——阿里贝尔特王子是在位王子吗？就是那种，我记得，你们欧洲人称作‘执政王子’（Prince Regnant）的？”

“殿下并非在位王子，而且多半永远也当不上，”迪莫克答道，“波森的大公宝座，如今由殿下的侄子欧根大公（Grand Duke Eugen）坐着。”

“侄子？”内拉惊叫一声。

“这有什么奇怪的呢，亲爱的女士？”

===Original===
“But Prince Aribert is surely very young?”

“The Prince, by one of those vagaries of chance which occur sometimes in the history of families, is precisely the same age as the Grand Duke. The late Grand Duke’s father was twice married. Hence this youthfulness on the part of an uncle.”

“How delicious to be the uncle of someone as old as yourself! But I suppose it is no fun for Prince Aribert. I suppose he has to be frightfully respectful and obedient, and all that, to his nephew?”

“The Grand Duke and my Serene master are like brothers. At present, of course, Prince Aribert is nominally heir to the throne, but as no doubt you are aware, the Grand Duke will shortly marry a near relative of the Emperor’s, and should there be a family—” Mr. Dimmock stopped and shrugged his straight shoulders. “The Grand Duke,” he went on, without finishing the last sentence, “would much prefer Prince Aribert to be his successor. He really doesn’t want to marry. Between ourselves, strictly between ourselves, he regards marriage as rather a bore. But, of course, being a German Grand Duke, he is bound to marry. He owes it to his country, to Posen.”

===Chinese===
“可是阿里贝尔特王子分明还很年轻啊？”

“由于家族史上时而出现的那类机缘乖谬，王子的年纪与大公恰好一般大。已故大公的父亲结过两次婚。于是便有了叔叔这般年轻的局面。”

“给一个跟自己同岁的人当叔叔，多有趣呀！不过我想这对阿里贝尔特王子未必有趣。我想他总得对那位侄子十二万分地恭敬顺从，诸如此类的吧？”

“大公和我家殿下亲如手足。眼下，阿里贝尔特王子名义上固然是王位继承人，不过想必二位也清楚，大公不久就要迎娶皇帝的一位近亲，一旦有了后嗣——”迪莫克先生说到此处停住，耸了耸他那笔直的肩膀。“大公呢，”他没有续完那句话，径自往下说，“其实巴不得由阿里贝尔特王子来继位。他实在不愿意结婚。咱们私下说，严格地私下说——他把婚姻看作一桩相当乏味的事。当然了，身为德意志大公，他非结婚不可。这是他对国家、对波森的义务。”

===Original===
“How large is Posen?” asked Racksole bluntly.

“Father,” Nella interposed laughing, “you shouldn’t ask such inconvenient questions. You ought to have guessed that it isn’t etiquette to inquire about the size of a German Dukedom.”

“I am sure,” said Dimmock, with a polite smile, “that the Grand Duke is as much amused as anyone at the size of his territory. I forget the exact acreage, but I remember that once Prince Aribert and myself walked across it and back again in a single day.”

===Chinese===
“波森有多大？”拉克索尔直截了当地问。

“爸爸，”内拉笑着插进来，“您不该问这种叫人为难的问题。您该猜得到，打听一个德意志公国的面积，是不合规矩的。”

“我可以担保，”迪莫克带着有礼的微笑说，“对自己领地之小，大公本人跟大家一样觉得有趣。确切亩数我记不得了，只记得有一回，阿里贝尔特王子和我花了一天工夫，把这片国土徒步横穿了一个来回。”

===Original===
“Then the Grand Duke cannot travel very far within his own dominions? You may say that the sun does set on his empire?”

“It does,” said Dimmock.

“Unless the weather is cloudy,” Nella put in. “Is the Grand Duke content always to stay at home?”

===Chinese===
“那么大公在自家领地里也走不了多远喽？您尽可以说，太阳在他的帝国上头是会落山的？”

“的确落，”迪莫克说。

“除非赶上阴天，”内拉插嘴道。“那位大公可甘心老是待在家里？”

===Original===
“On the contrary, he is a great traveller, much more so than Prince Aribert. I may tell you, what no one knows at present, outside this hotel, that his Royal Highness the Grand Duke, with a small suite, will be here tomorrow.”

“In London?” asked Nella.

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“恰恰相反，他是个大旅行家，比阿里贝尔特王子旅行得多多。我可以告诉你一件眼下这座饭店之外无人知晓的事：大公殿下明天将带着一小队随从驾临此地。”

“到伦敦来？”内拉问。

“对。”

===Original===
“In this hotel?”

“Yes.”

“Oh! How lovely!”

“That is why your humble servant is here tonight—a sort of advance guard.”

===Chinese===
“住这家饭店？”

“对。”

“哦！太叫人高兴了！”

“鄙人今晚之所以在此，正是为此——算个先遣人员吧。”

===Original===
“But I understood,” Racksole said, “that you were—er—attached to Prince Aribert, the uncle.”

“I am. Prince Aribert will also be here. The Grand Duke and the Prince have business about important investments connected with the Grand Duke’s marriage settlement. … In the highest quarters, you understand.”

“For so discreet a person,” thought Racksole, “you are fairly communicative.” Then he said aloud: “Shall we go out on the terrace?”

===Chinese===
“可我听说的，”拉克索尔说，“你是——呃——跟着当叔叔的阿里贝尔特王子的呀。”

“我是跟着他。阿里贝尔特王子也会来。大公和王子有点事情要办，同大公婚姻财产协议（marriage settlement）相关的一笔重要投资。……都是顶高层的事，你明白。”

“像你这样谨言慎行的人，”拉克索尔心中暗想，“倒相当健谈。”随后他高声道：“我们到露台上去，如何？”

===Original===
As they crossed the dining-room Jules stopped Mr. Dimmock and handed him a letter. “Just come, sir, by messenger,” said Jules.

Nella dropped behind for a second with her father. “Leave me alone with this boy a little—there’s a dear parent,” she whispered in his ear.

“I am a mere cipher, an obedient nobody,” Racksole replied, pinching her arm surreptitiously. “Treat me as such. Use me as you like. I will go and look after my hotel.” And soon afterwards he disappeared.

===Chinese===
穿过餐厅的时候，朱尔拦住迪莫克先生，递给他一封信。“刚送到的，先生，专人送来的。”朱尔说。

内拉落后半步，偕着父亲。“让我跟这小子单独待一会儿——才是我的好爸爸。”她贴着他耳朵悄声说。

“我不过是个可有可无的零，一个听话的小人物，”拉克索尔答道，一面偷偷捏了捏她的胳膊。“你就拿我当这种人。爱怎么差遣怎么差遣。我去看我的饭店了。”不多时他便没了踪影。

===Original===
Nella and Mr. Dimmock sat together on the terrace, sipping iced drinks. They made a handsome couple, bowered amid plants which blossomed at the command of a Chelsea wholesale florist. People who passed by remarked privately that from the look of things there was the beginning of a romance in that conversation. Perhaps there was, but a more intimate acquaintance with the character of Nella Racksole would have been necessary in order to predict what precise form that romance would take.

Jules himself served the liquids, and at ten o’clock he brought another note. Entreating a thousand pardons, Reginald Dimmock, after he had glanced at the note, excused himself on the plea of urgent business for his Serene master, uncle of the Grand Duke of Posen. He asked if he might fetch Mr. Racksole, or escort Miss Racksole to her father. But Miss Racksole said gaily that she felt no need of an escort, and should go to bed. She added that her father and herself always endeavoured to be independent of each other.

Just then Theodore Racksole had found his way once more into Mr. Babylon’s private room. Before arriving there, however, he had discovered that in some mysterious manner the news of the change of proprietorship had worked its way down to the lowest strata of the hotel’s cosmos. The corridors hummed with it, and even under-servants were to be seen discussing the thing, just as though it mattered to them.

===Chinese===
内拉和迪莫克先生并肩坐在露台上，啜着冰饮。两人凑成很登对的一双，掩映在花木搭成的凉棚里——那些花木全是听了切尔西（Chelsea）一家花卉批发行的号令才开放的。路过的人私下纷纷说，瞧这情形，那段谈话里正萌出一段罗曼史的苗头。兴许真有；不过，要想预知这段罗曼史究竟会长成什么模样，还得先对内拉·拉克索尔的性格有更深的相识才成。

酒水由朱尔亲自侍候；十点钟，他又送来一张便条。雷金纳德·迪莫克瞥了便条一眼，连声道着万分抱歉，便以他家殿下——波森大公的那位叔叔——有急务在身为由，告退了。他问可否去请拉克索尔先生来，或是护送拉克索尔小姐到她父亲那里去。拉克索尔小姐却欢然说道，她并不需要什么护送，这就回房安歇。她还添了一句：她同她父亲向来力求彼此各不相扰。

就在这时，西奥多·拉克索尔已经再一次摸进了巴比伦先生（Mr. Babylon）的私室。不过，在到达那里之前，他已然发现，易主的消息不知经由什么神秘的途径，一路渗到了这座饭店小宇宙的最底层。走廊里嗡嗡地全是这件事，连下等仆役都可见三五成堆地议论，仿佛这事与他们有多大的干系。

===Original===
“Have a cigar, Mr. Racksole,” said the urbane Mr. Babylon, “and a mouthful of the oldest cognac in all Europe.”

In a few minutes these two were talking eagerly, rapidly. Félix Babylon was astonished at Racksole’s capacity for absorbing the details of hotel management. And as for Racksole he soon realized that Félix Babylon must be a prince of hotel managers. It had never occurred to Racksole before that to manage an hotel, even a large hotel, could be a specially interesting affair, or that it could make any excessive demands upon the brains of the manager; but he came to see that he had underrated the possibilities of an hotel. The business of the Grand Babylon was enormous. It took Racksole, with all his genius for organization, exactly half an hour to master the details of the hotel laundry-work. And the laundry-work was but one branch of activity amid scores, and not a very large one at that. The machinery of checking supplies, and of establishing a mean ratio between the raw stuff received in the kitchen and the number of meals served in the salle-à-manger and the private rooms, was very complicated and delicate. When Racksole had grasped it, he at once suggested some improvements, and this led to a long theoretical discussion, and the discussion led to digressions, and then Félix Babylon, in a moment of absentmindedness, yawned.

Racksole looked at the gilt clock on the high mantelpiece.

===Chinese===
“来一支雪茄吧，拉克索尔先生，”一团和气的巴比伦先生说，“再呷一口全欧洲陈得最久的干邑。”

不出几分钟，这两人便谈得热火而急切。费利克斯·巴比伦对拉克索尔吞纳饭店管理细目的本事大为惊异；至于拉克索尔，他很快便省悟到，费利克斯·巴比伦必定是饭店经理人中的王侯。拉克索尔先前从未想到过，经营一家饭店——哪怕是家大饭店——竟会是件特别有味的事，也竟会对经理的脑力提出这般苛刻的要求；但他随即看出，自己低估了一家饭店的可能性。大巴比伦的生意规模惊人。凭着拉克索尔那套组织的天才，单是掌握饭店洗衣房的种种细目，就整整花了半个钟头。而洗衣房不过是几十个业务部门当中的一支，还算不得很大的一支。核验进货、在厨房收进的原料与餐厅（salle-à-manger）和各处雅间开出的餐数之间定出平均比率的这套机制，极其繁复精微。拉克索尔一把它弄通，立即提出若干改良，由此引出一场漫长的理论讨论，讨论又荡开去各处题外，而后，费利克斯·巴比伦在出神的一瞬，打了个哈欠。

拉克索尔望了望高高的壁炉台上那座镀金的钟。

===Original===
“Great Scott!” he said. “It’s three o’clock. Mr. Babylon, accept my apologies for having kept you up to such an absurd hour.”

“I have not spent so pleasant an evening for many years. You have let me ride my hobby to my heart’s content. It is I who should apologize.”

Racksole rose.

===Chinese===
“我的老天！”他说，“三点了。巴比伦先生，容我把你拖到这么个荒唐的钟点，务请接受我的道歉。”

“好多年没度过这么称心的夜晚了。你容我把心头那匹癖好之马骑了个尽兴。该道歉的是我才是。”

拉克索尔起身。

===Original===
“I should like to ask you one question,” said Babylon. “Have you ever had anything to do with hotels before?”

“Never,” said Racksole.

“Then you have missed your vocation. You could have been the greatest of all hotel-managers. You would have been greater than me, and I am unequalled, though I keep only one hotel, and some men have half a dozen. Mr. Racksole, why have you never run an hotel?”

===Chinese===
“我想问你一个问题，”巴比伦说。“你先前同饭店打过交道么？”

“从来没有，”拉克索尔说。

“那么你是错失了你的天职。你本可以成为一切饭店经理人中最伟大的一个。你会比我更伟大——而我已是无可匹敌，尽管我只开一家饭店，有的人手上却有五六家。拉克索尔先生，你为什么从不经营饭店？”

===Original===
“Heaven knows,” he laughed, “but you flatter me, Mr. Babylon.”

“I? Flatter? You do not know me. I flatter no one, except, perhaps, now and then an exceptionally distinguished guest. In which case I give suitable instructions as to the bill.”

“Speaking of distinguished guests, I am told that a couple of German princes are coming here tomorrow.”

“That is so.”

===Chinese===
“天晓得，”他笑道，“不过你是过奖了，巴比伦先生。”

“我？过奖？你还不认识我。我谁也不恭维——也许偶尔破例，对一位格外显贵的客人。那种时候，我会在账单上作出相应的吩咐。”

“说起显贵的客人——听说有两位德意志王子明天要来此地。”

“确有其事。”

===Original===
“Does one do anything? Does one receive them formally—stand bowing in the entrance-hall, or anything of that sort?”

“Not necessarily. Not unless one wishes. The modern hotel proprietor is not like an innkeeper of the Middle Ages, and even princes do not expect to see him unless something should happen to go wrong. As a matter of fact, though the Grand Duke of Posen and Prince Aribert have both honoured me by staying here before, I have never even set eyes on them. You will find all arrangements have been made.”

They talked a little longer, and then Racksole said good night. “Let me see you to your room. The lifts will be closed and the place will be deserted. As for myself, I sleep here,” and Mr. Babylon pointed to an inner door.

===Chinese===
“那么要不要做点什么？要不要正式接驾——站在门厅里躬身相迎，诸如此类？”

“不一定。自己不想就不必。现代的饭店老板已不是中世纪的客栈掌柜，即便王子，除非恰巧出了什么岔子，也并不指望见到老板。实际上，波森大公和阿里贝尔特王子先前都曾赏光下榻于此，我却连他们的面也未曾见过。你会发现一切安排早已齐备。”

两人又谈了一会儿，拉克索尔便道了晚安。“让我送你回房。电梯要停了，四下里也会空无一人。至于我，就睡在这里。”巴比伦先生指着一扇内门说。

===Original===
“No, thanks,” said Racksole; “let me explore my own hotel unaccompanied. I believe I can discover my room.” When he got fairly into the passages, Racksole was not so sure that he could discover his own room. The number was 107, but he had forgotten whether it was on the first or second floor.

Travelling in a lift, one is unconscious of floors. He passed several lift-doorways, but he could see no glint of a staircase; in all self-respecting hotels staircases have gone out of fashion, and though hotel architects still continue, for old sakes’ sake, to build staircases, they are tucked away in remote corners where their presence is not likely to offend the eye of a spoiled and cosmopolitan public. The hotel seemed vast, uncanny, deserted. An electric light glowed here and there at long intervals. On the thick carpets, Racksole’s thinly-shod feet made no sound, and he wandered at ease to and fro, rather amused, rather struck by the peculiar senses of night and mystery which had suddenly come over him. He fancied he could hear a thousand snores peacefully descending from the upper realms. At length he found a staircase, a very dark and narrow one, and presently he was on the first floor. He soon discovered that the numbers of the rooms on this floor did not get beyond seventy. He encountered another staircase and ascended to the second floor. By the decoration of the walls he recognized this floor as his proper home, and as he strolled through the long corridor he whistled a low, meditative whistle of satisfaction. He thought he heard a step in the transverse corridor, and instinctively he obliterated himself in a recess which held a service-cabinet and a chair. He did hear a step. Peeping cautiously out, he perceived, what he had not perceived previously, that a piece of white ribbon had been tied round the handle of the door of one of the bedrooms. Then a man came round the corner of the transverse corridor, and Racksole drew back. It was Jules—Jules with his hands in his pockets and a slouch hat over his eyes, but in other respects attired as usual.

===Chinese===
“不用了，多谢，”拉克索尔说，“容我独自去探一探自家的饭店。我想我找得到自己的房间。”待到真正走进那些甬道，拉克索尔却没这么大把握了。房间是 107 号，可他忘了是在一楼还是二楼。

乘电梯的人，是不知楼层为何物的。他走过几处电梯门厅，却望不见楼梯的一星影子；凡自重身份的饭店，楼梯早已不时髦了，饭店建筑师虽仍看在旧情的份上继续造楼梯，也都把它们收进偏僻角落，免得碍了娇纵成性、周游列国的看客们的眼目。这座饭店显得浩大、诡谲、空寂。电灯隔着老长的间隔，这里那里亮着一盏。厚地毯上，拉克索尔那双薄底鞋悄然无声，他自在地来回游荡，颇觉有趣，又颇被那突如其来的夜与神秘的奇异感觉所震动。他恍然听见千百声鼾息，正从上界安然飘下。末了他寻见一道楼梯，又暗又窄的一道，不多时便到了一楼。他随即发现，这一层的房号都过不了七十。又碰上一道楼梯，他便升到二楼。凭墙上的装潢，他认出这一层才是自己的本宅。他在长廊里信步而行，低低吹出一声若有所思的口哨，颇为惬意。忽然他仿佛听见横廊里有脚步声，便本能地把自身抹进一处凹壁，里头放着一只服务柜和一把椅子。脚步声是确凿的。他谨慎地向外窥探，这才看出先前未曾看出的一桩事：一间卧室的门把手上，系着一条白缎带。随后一个男人从横廊拐角转过身来，拉克索尔缩了回去。是朱尔——朱尔双手插在衣兜里，一顶软呢帽低低压住眼睛，除此之外，装束一如平日。

===Original===
Racksole, at that instant, remembered with a special vividness what Félix Babylon had said to him at their first interview. He wished he had brought his revolver. He didn’t know why he should feel the desirability of a revolver in a London hotel of the most unimpeachable fair fame, but he did feel the desirability of such an instrument of attack and defence. He privately decided that if Jules went past his recess he would take him by the throat and in that attitude put a few plain questions to this highly dubious waiter. But Jules had stopped. The millionaire made another cautious observation. Jules, with infinite gentleness, was turning the handle of the door to which the white ribbon was attached. The door slowly yielded and Jules disappeared within the room. After a brief interval, the night-prowling Jules reappeared, closed the door as softly as he had opened it, removed the ribbon, returned upon his steps, and vanished down the transverse corridor.

===Chinese===
那一瞬间，拉克索尔格外鲜明地记起了初次会面时费利克斯·巴比伦对他说过的话。他懊悔没把左轮手枪带来。他也说不上为什么，在一座名声清白得无可指摘的伦敦饭店里，竟会觉出左轮手枪之可欲；可他确实觉出这么一件攻防两用的利器之可欲。他暗下决心：倘若朱尔打他这凹壁前经过，他就扼住此人的咽喉，就着这个架势，向这位十分可疑的侍者盘问几句直白话。然而朱尔停住了。百万富翁又谨慎窥察了一番。朱尔正以无限的轻柔，转动那条系着白缎带的门的把手。门缓缓地让开了，朱尔隐入房中。片刻之后，这夜间游荡的朱尔重新现身，把门关得同开时一般轻，摘去缎带，循原路折返，消失在横廊深处。

===Original===
“This is quaint,” said Racksole; “quaint to a degree!”

It occurred to him to look at the number of the room, and he stole towards it.

“Well, I’m damned!” he murmured wonderingly.

The number was 111, his daughter’s room! He tried to open it, but the door was locked. Rushing to his own room, No. 107, he seized one of a pair of revolvers (the kind that are made for millionaires) and followed after Jules down the transverse corridor. At the end of this corridor was a window; the window was open; and Jules was innocently gazing out of the window. Ten silent strides, and Theodore Racksole was upon him.

===Chinese===
“这可真古怪，”拉克索尔说，“古怪到了十二分！”

他起意看看那房间的号码，便蹑足凑近前去。

“好家伙，撞见鬼了！”他诧异地喃喃道。

那正是 111 号——他女儿的房间！他试着开门，门锁着。他奔回自己的 107 号房，抓起成对手枪中的一把（专为百万富翁制造的那一种），顺着横廊追踪朱尔而去。廊的尽头有一扇窗；窗开着；朱尔正一脸无辜地凭窗外望。十个无声的大步，西奥多·拉克索尔已逼到他身后。

===Original===
“One word, my friend,” the millionaire began, carelessly waving the revolver in the air. Jules was indubitably startled, but by an admirable exercise of self-control he recovered possession of his faculties in a second.

“Sir?” said Jules.

“I just want to be informed, what the deuce you were doing in No. 111 a moment ago.”

“I had been requested to go there,” was the calm response.

===Chinese===
“借一句话，我的朋友，”百万富翁开腔了，一边满不在乎地把左轮手枪朝空中挥了挥。朱尔无疑吃了一惊，但凭着一手可敬的自我克制，一秒钟便收回了心神。

“先生？”朱尔说。

“我只想知道个明白，你方才究竟在 111 号房里捣的什么鬼。”

“是有人要我到那儿去的。”回答得四平八稳。

===Original===
“You are a liar, and not a very clever one. That is my daughter’s room. Now—out with it, before I decide whether to shoot you or throw you into the street.”

“Excuse me, sir, No. 111 is occupied by a gentleman.”

“I advise you that it is a serious error of judgement to contradict me, my friend. Don’t do it again. We will go to the room together, and you shall prove that the occupant is a gentleman, and not my daughter.”

“Impossible, sir,” said Jules.

===Chinese===
“你撒谎，而且撒得不甚高明。那是我女儿的房间。现在——统统吐出来，趁我还没决定是毙了你，还是把你扔到街上去。”

“恕我回禀，先生，111 号房住的是一位绅士。”

“我奉劝你，我的朋友，跟我顶牛是个严重的判断错误。下回别这样。我们一同到那房间去，你来向我证明房客是位绅士，而不是我的女儿。”

“这办不到，先生，”朱尔说。

===Original===
“Scarcely that,” said Racksole, and he took Jules by the sleeve. The millionaire knew for a certainty that Nella occupied No. 111, for he had examined the room with her, and himself seen that her trunks and her maid and herself had arrived there in safety. “Now open the door,” whispered Racksole, when they reached No. 111.

“I must knock.”

“That is just what you mustn’t do. Open it. No doubt you have your passkey.”

Confronted by the revolver, Jules readily obeyed, yet with a deprecatory gesture, as though he would not be responsible for this outrage against the decorum of hotel life. Racksole entered. The room was brilliantly lighted.

===Chinese===
“岂止是办不到，”拉克索尔说着，一把攥住朱尔的袖口。百万富翁千真万确地知道内拉住的是 111 号，因为那房间是他陪她一同检视定下的，她的衣箱、她的女仆连同她本人，都是他亲眼看着平安抵达的。“现在开门。”到了 111 号门前，拉克索尔低声说。

“我必须敲一敲门。”

“你偏偏就是不能敲。开。你身上无疑带着万能钥匙。”

在手枪的逼视之下，朱尔爽快从命，却做出了一个不胜歉疚的手势，仿佛这种冒犯饭店生活体统的暴行，他一概不肯负责。拉克索尔跨了进去。房间里灯火通明。

===Original===
“A visitor, who insists on seeing you, sir,” said Jules, and fled.

Mr. Reginald Dimmock, still in evening dress, and smoking a cigarette, rose hurriedly from a table.

“Hello, my dear Mr. Racksole, this is an unexpected—ah—pleasure.”

===Chinese===
“有一位访客，执意要见您，先生。”朱尔说完，一溜烟跑了。

雷金纳德·迪莫克先生仍旧一身晚礼服，指间夹着香烟，慌忙从一张桌旁立起身来。

“哎呀呀，我亲爱的拉克索尔先生，这真是一场料想不到的——呃——幸会。”

===Original===
“Where is my daughter? This is her room.”

“Did I catch what you said, Mr. Racksole?”

“I venture to remark that this is Miss Racksole’s room.”

“My good sir,” answered Dimmock, “you must be mad to dream of such a thing. Only my respect for your daughter prevents me from expelling you forcibly, for such an extraordinary suggestion.”

===Chinese===
“我女儿在哪儿？这是她的房间。”

“拉克索尔先生，我没听岔吧，您方才说什么？”

“我斗胆指出：这里是拉克索尔小姐的房间。”

“我的好先生，”迪莫克答道，“梦里都冒出这种念头来，您准是疯了。只因敬重您的女儿，我才没有强行把您架出去——就为这么一句匪夷所思的话。”

===Original===
A small spot halfway down the bridge of the millionaire’s nose turned suddenly white.

“With your permission,” he said in a low calm voice, “I will examine the dressing-room and the bathroom.”

“Just listen to me a moment,” Dimmock urged, in a milder tone.

“I’ll listen to you afterwards, my young friend,” said Racksole, and he proceeded to search the bathroom, and the dressing-room, without any result whatever. “Lest my attitude might be open to misconstruction, Mr. Dimmock, I may as well tell you that I have the most perfect confidence in my daughter, who is as well able to take care of herself as any woman I ever met, but since you entered it there have been one or two rather mysterious occurrences in this hotel. That is all.” Feeling a draught of air on his shoulder, Racksole turned to the window. “For instance,” he added, “I perceive that this window is broken, badly broken, and from the outside. Now, how could that have occurred?”

===Chinese===
百万富翁鼻梁半腰上的一个小点，霎时白了。

“承您俯允，”他用低沉而平静的声音说，“我要查看更衣室和浴室。”

“您先容我说一句，”迪莫克换了温和些的口气催道。

“回头再听你讲，我的年轻朋友。”拉克索尔说罢，便动手搜查浴室和更衣室，全然一无所获。“免得我的态度遭人误解，迪莫克先生，我不妨明告：我对我的女儿有十足的信心，论照管自己的本事，她不输于我平生所见的任何女子；可是打你住进这座饭店以来，已经出了一两桩相当蹊跷的事。如此而已。”肩上觉出一阵穿堂风，拉克索尔转向窗子。“比方说，”他补上一句，“我注意到这扇窗破了，破得很厉害，而且是从外面打破的。那么，请问这怎么会发生呢？”

===Original===
“If you will kindly hear reason, Mr. Racksole,” said Dimmock in his best diplomatic manner, “I will endeavour to explain things to you. I regarded your first question to me when you entered my room as being offensively put, but I now see that you had some justification.” He smiled politely. “I was passing along this corridor about eleven o’clock, when I found Miss Racksole in a difficulty with the hotel servants. Miss Racksole was retiring to rest in this room when a large stone, which must have been thrown from the Embankment, broke the window, as you see. Apart from the discomfort of the broken window, she did not care to remain in the room. She argued that where one stone had come another might follow. She therefore insisted on her room being changed. The servants said that there was no other room available with a dressing-room and bathroom attached, and your daughter made a point of these matters. I at once offered to exchange apartments with her. She did me the honour to accept my offer. Our respective belongings were moved—and that is all. Miss Racksole is at this moment, I trust, asleep in No. 124.”

===Chinese===
“您若是肯赏脸听听道理，拉克索尔先生，”迪莫克拿出他最拿手的外交派头说，“我便尽力把事情向您解释明白。您方才进我房间时向我提的头一个问题，我原本认为问得颇有冒犯，不过如今看来，您自有几分道理。”他客气地微笑。“大约十一点钟，我正沿这条廊子走过，撞见拉克索尔小姐同饭店仆役相持不下。拉克索尔小姐原要在这间房里安寝，忽然一块大石头——准是从河堤（the Embankment）上掷来的——砸破了窗子，正如您眼见的样子。撇开破窗的种种不便不谈，她也不愿再留在这间房里。她的说法是：一块石头既来过了，第二块兴许跟着就来。于是她坚执要换房间。仆役说，带更衣室和浴室的房间再无别的空余，而您的女儿偏偏认定这两样少不得。我当即提出与她互换房间。她赏了我的脸，应允了。彼此的行李搬动了一遍——如此而已。拉克索尔小姐此刻，但愿，正在 124 号安睡。”

===Original===
Theodore Racksole looked at the young man for a few seconds in silence.

There was a faint knock at the door.

“Come in,” said Racksole loudly.

Someone pushed open the door, but remained standing on the mat. It was Nella’s maid, in a dressing-gown.

“Miss Racksole’s compliments, and a thousand excuses, but a book of hers was left on the mantelshelf in this room. She cannot sleep, and wishes to read.”

===Chinese===
西奥多·拉克索尔一声不响地端详了这年轻人几秒钟。

门上响起一下轻轻的叩击。

“进来。”拉克索尔朗声道。

有人推开门，却只站在门垫上不曾进来。是内拉的女仆，穿着一件睡袍。

“拉克索尔小姐向您致意，并万分告罪：她有一本书遗忘在这间房的壁炉架上了。她睡不着，想看点书。”

===Original===
“Mr. Dimmock, I tender my apologies—my formal apologies,” said Racksole, when the girl had gone away with the book. “Good night.”

“Pray don’t mention it,” said Dimmock suavely—and bowed him out.
===Chinese===
待那姑娘拿了书走后——“迪莫克先生，我向你致歉——郑重其事的道歉，”拉克索尔说，“晚安。”

“千万不必挂在心上，”迪莫克温文有礼地说——随即躬身送他出门。

## IV Entrance of the Prince / IV 王子登场

===Original===
Nevertheless, sundry small things weighed on Racksole’s mind. First there was Jules’ wink. Then there was the ribbon on the door-handle and Jules’ visit to No. 111, and the broken window﻿—broken from the outside. Racksole did not forget that the time was 3 a.m. He slept but little that night, but he was glad that he had bought the Grand Babylon Hotel. It was an acquisition which seemed to promise fun and diversion.

The next morning he came across Mr. Babylon early. “I have emptied my private room of all personal papers,” said Babylon, “and it is now at your disposal. I purpose, if agreeable to yourself, to stay on in the hotel as a guest for the present. We have much to settle with regard to the completion of the purchase, and also there are things which you might want to ask me. Also, to tell the truth, I am not anxious to leave the old place with too much suddenness. It will be a wrench to me.”

“I shall be delighted if you will stay,” said the millionaire, “but it must be as my guest, not as the guest of the hotel.”

“You are very kind.”

===Chinese===
尽管如此，仍有几桩小事压在拉克索尔（Racksole）的心头。头一件，是朱尔（Jules）使的那个眼色。其次是门把手上系的那条缎带、朱尔去 111 号房的那一趟，还有那扇破掉的窗户——是从外面打破的。拉克索尔没有忘记，那会儿已是凌晨三点。这一夜他睡得很少，不过他心里高兴：自己到底把大巴比伦饭店（the Grand Babylon Hotel）买了下来。这桩到手的产业，看着就许下了乐趣和消遣。

第二天一早，他就碰见了巴比伦先生（Mr. Babylon）。“我已把自己专用办公室里的私人文件清理一空，”巴比伦说，“如今它听凭你支配。如蒙首肯，我打算暂且以一名住客的身份继续留在店里。买卖收尾还有许多事要料理；再说，你兴许还有些事想问我。况且，说句实话，我也不愿太突然地离开这老地方。骤然撒手，我会舍不得的。”

“你肯留下，我求之不得，”这位百万富翁说，“不过得以我的客人身份留下，不能算饭店的客人。”

“你太客气了。”

===Original===
“As for wishing to consult you, no doubt I shall have need to do so, but I must say that the show seems to run itself.”

“Ah!” said Babylon thoughtfully. “I have heard of hotels that run themselves. If they do, you may be sure that they obey the laws of gravity and run downwards. You will have your hands full. For example, have you yet heard about Miss Spencer?”

“No,” said Racksole. “What of her?”

“She has mysteriously vanished during the night, and nobody appears to be able to throw any light on the affair. Her room is empty, her boxes gone. You will want someone to take her place, and that someone will not be very easy to get.”

===Chinese===
“至于说要向你讨教，无疑免不了要如此。不过我得说，这摊子买卖瞧着倒像自己会运转。”

“啊！”巴比伦若有所思地说。“自己会运转的饭店，我倒是听说过。倘若真有，那它们准是服从万有引力定律，一路往下运转。你有得忙了。比方说，斯宾塞小姐（Miss Spencer）的事，你可曾听说？”

“没有，”拉克索尔说。“她怎么了？”

“她夜里神秘地失踪了，似乎没有谁能为这件事提供一点线索。她的房间空了，箱子也不见了。你会需要有人顶替她的位置，而那个人可不容易找到。”

===Original===
“H’m!” Racksole said, after a pause. “Hers is not the only post that falls vacant today.”

A little later, the millionaire installed himself in the late owner’s private room and rang the bell.

“I want Jules,” he said to the page.

While waiting for Jules, Racksole considered the question of Miss Spencer’s disappearance.

===Chinese===
“唔！”拉克索尔顿了顿，说道。“今天出缺的位置，可不止她这一个。”

过了一会儿，这位百万富翁在原东家的专用办公室里安顿下来，按了按铃。

“我要朱尔。”他对门童说。

等朱尔的工夫，拉克索尔琢磨着斯宾塞小姐失踪这件事。

===Original===
“Good morning, Jules,” was his cheerful greeting, when the imperturbable waiter arrived.

“Good morning, sir.”

“Take a chair.”

“Thank you, sir.”

“We have met before this morning, Jules.”

===Chinese===
那位不动声色的领班来到时，他兴致勃勃地招呼道：“早安，朱尔。”

“早安，先生。”

“拉把椅子坐吧。”

“谢谢您，先生。”

“今天早上咱们已经碰过一面了，朱尔。”

===Original===
“Yes, sir, at 3 a.m.”

“Rather strange about Miss Spencer’s departure, is it not?” suggested Racksole.

“It is remarkable, sir.”

“You are aware, of course, that Mr. Babylon has transferred all his interests in this hotel to me?”

“I have been informed to that effect, sir.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生，凌晨三点。”

“斯宾塞小姐的离开挺蹊跷的，你说是不是？”拉克索尔试探道。

“确实非同寻常，先生。”

“巴比伦先生已把他在本店的全部权益转给了我，这你当然知道？”

“我已接到这方面的知会，先生。”

===Original===
“I suppose you know everything that goes on in the hotel, Jules?”

“As the head waiter, sir, it is my business to keep a general eye on things.”

“You speak very good English for a foreigner, Jules.”

“For a foreigner, sir! I am an Englishman, a Hertfordshire man born and bred. Perhaps my name has misled you, sir. I am only called Jules because the head waiter of any really high-class hotel must have either a French or an Italian name.”

“I see,” said Racksole. “I think you must be rather a clever person, Jules.”

===Chinese===
“我想，饭店里发生的事没有你不知道的吧，朱尔？”

“身为领班，先生，眼观全局乃是分内之责。”

“以一个外国人而论，你的英语讲得相当好，朱尔。”

“还说外国人呢，先生！我是英国人，土生土长的赫特福德郡（Hertfordshire）人。兴许是敝名让您误会了，先生。人家叫我朱尔，只因为但凡真正高等的饭店，领班非得有个法国名或意大利名不可。”

“原来如此，”拉克索尔说。“我看你这个人想必相当精明，朱尔。”

===Original===
“That is not for me to say, sir.”

“How long has the hotel enjoyed the advantage of your services?”

“A little over twenty years.”

“That is a long time to be in one place. Don’t you think it’s time you got out of the rut? You are still young, and might make a reputation for yourself in another and wider sphere.”

===Chinese===
“这可轮不到我自己来说，先生。”

“本店享你效劳之惠，有多久了？”

“二十年多一点。”

“待在一个地方这么久，可不算短了。你不觉得到了跳出这趟老套子的时候吗？你还年轻，换一个更宽的天地，未必不能给自己挣出名声来。”

===Original===
Racksole looked at the man steadily, and his glance was steadily returned.

“You aren’t satisfied with me, sir?”

“To be frank, Jules, I think﻿—I think you﻿—er﻿—wink too much. And I think that it is regrettable when a head waiter falls into a habit of taking white ribbons from the handles of bedroom doors at three in the morning.”

Jules started slightly.

===Chinese===
拉克索尔目不转睛地盯着此人，对方也毫不躲闪地迎着他的目光。

“您对我不满意了，是吗，先生？”

“实话说吧，朱尔，我觉得——我觉得你——呃——眼色使得太勤。而且我认为，一位领班要是养成习惯，凌晨三点去摘卧室门把上的白缎带，那可是件令人遗憾的事。”

朱尔微微一怔。

===Original===
“I see how it is, sir. You wish me to go, and one pretext, if I may use the term, is as good as another. Very well, I can’t say that I’m surprised. It sometimes happens that there is incompatibility of temper between a hotel proprietor and his head waiter, and then, unless one of them goes, the hotel is likely to suffer. I will go, Mr. Racksole. In fact, I had already thought of giving notice.”

The millionaire smiled appreciatively. “What wages do you require in lieu of notice? It is my intention that you leave the hotel within an hour.”

“I require no wages in lieu of notice, sir. I would scorn to accept anything. And I will leave the hotel in fifteen minutes.”

“Good day, then. You have my good wishes and my admiration, so long as you keep out of my hotel.”

===Chinese===
“我明白是怎么回事了，先生。您要我走，那么随便哪个借口——恕我斗胆用这个词——都一样好使。很好，我不能说自己吃了一惊。饭店老板和他的领班之间，有时难免性情不合；到了那一步，两人里若不走掉一个，饭店多半要跟着遭殃。我这就走，拉克索尔先生。其实，我早已经在打算递辞呈了。”

百万富翁会心地一笑。“代替预告期的补偿工钱，你要多少？我的意思，是要你一个钟头之内离开这家饭店。”

“补偿工钱我分文不要，先生。接受什么我都觉得掉价。十五分钟之内我就离开饭店。”

“那么，再会。只要你别再踏进我这家饭店，我的祝福和敬佩就都归你。”

===Original===
Racksole got up. “Good day, sir. And thank you.”

“By the way, Jules, it will be useless for you to apply to any other first-rate European hotel for a post, because I shall take measures which will ensure the rejection of any such application.”

“Without discussing the question whether or not there aren’t at least half a dozen hotels in London alone that would jump for joy at the chance of getting me,” answered Jules, “I may tell you, sir, that I shall retire from my profession.”

“Really! You will turn your brains to a different channel.”

===Chinese===
拉克索尔站起身。“再会，先生。谢谢您。”

“对了，还有一句，朱尔：你去欧洲别的任何一家一流饭店谋职都是白费劲，因为我会采取措施，保证这类申请必遭回绝。”

“且不论光是伦敦一地，少说便有半打饭店能得到我就会乐得跳起来——这个问题不必争论——先生，我只需告诉您一句：我将从这一行里引退。”

“是嘛！那你的脑筋要换个渠道去转了。”

===Original===
“No, sir. I shall take rooms in Albemarle Street or Jermyn Street, and just be content to be a man-about-town. I have saved some twenty thousand pounds﻿—a mere trifle, but sufficient for my needs, and I shall now proceed to enjoy it. Pardon me for troubling you with my personal affairs. And good day again.”

That afternoon Racksole went with Félix Babylon first to a firm of solicitors in the City, and then to a stockbroker, in order to carry out the practical details of the purchase of the hotel.

“I mean to settle in England,” said Racksole, as they were coming back. “It is the only country﻿—” and he stopped.

“The only country?”

===Chinese===
“不，先生。我会在阿尔伯马尔街（Albemarle Street）或杰明街（Jermyn Street）租几间屋子，甘心做个上流闲人（man-about-town）。我攒下了两万镑上下——区区小数，却也够我所需，如今正要拿它来好好享用。拿这些私事来烦扰您，还望恕罪。那就再次再会了。”

当天下午，拉克索尔偕同费利克斯·巴比伦（Félix Babylon），先去伦敦城（the City）的一家律师事务所，又去找了一位股票经纪人，把购买饭店的各项实际手续一一办妥。

“我打算在英国定居，”两人往回走时，拉克索尔说，“这是唯一一个——”话到一半，他收住了。

“唯一一个什么？”

===Original===
“The only country where you can invest money and spend money with a feeling of security. In the United States there is nothing worth spending money on, nothing to buy. In France or Italy, there is no real security.”

“But surely you are a true American?” questioned Babylon.

“I am a true American,” said Racksole, “but my father, who began by being a bedmaker at an Oxford college, and ultimately made ten million dollars out of iron in Pittsburg﻿—my father took the wise precaution of having me educated in England. I had my three years at Oxford, like any son of the upper middle class! It did me good. It has been worth more to me than many successful speculations. It taught me that the English language is different from, and better than, the American language, and that there is something﻿—I haven’t yet found out exactly what﻿—in English life that Americans will never get. Why,” he added, “in the United States we still bribe our judges and our newspapers. And we talk of the eighteenth century as though it was the beginning of the world. Yes, I shall transfer my securities to London. I shall build a house in Park Lane, and I shall buy some immemorial country seat with a history as long as the A. T. and S. railroad, and I shall calmly and gradually settle down. D’you know﻿—I am rather a good-natured man for a millionaire, and of a social disposition, and yet I haven’t six real friends in the whole of New York City. Think of that!”

“And I,” said Babylon, “have no friends except the friends of my boyhood in Lausanne. I have spent thirty years in England, and gained nothing but a perfect knowledge of the English language and as much gold coin as would fill a rather large box.”

===Chinese===
“唯一一个你能既放心投资、又放心花钱的国家。在美国，没有什么值得花钱，也没有东西可买。在法国或意大利，没有真正的安全感。”

“可您终究是个地地道道的美国人吧？”巴比伦问道。

“我是个地地道道的美国人，”拉克索尔说，“不过我父亲——他起先在牛津的一所学院里当收拾寝室的杂役，末了在匹兹堡（Pittsburg）做铁业挣下了一千万美元——我父亲深谋远虑，早作安排，让我在英国受了教育。我跟随便哪个中上层人家的儿子一样，在牛津念满了三年！这对我大有裨益，比多少桩得手的投机买卖加在一起还值钱。它教会我，英语跟美语是两回事，而且英语高出一筹；它还教会我，英国人的生活里有某种东西——我至今没弄明白究竟是什么——是美国人永远得不到的。可不是，”他补上一句，“在美国，我们至今还在贿赂法官和报馆。我们把十八世纪说得仿佛那就是世界的开端。对，我要把证券转到伦敦来，要在公园巷（Park Lane）盖一幢宅子，再买下一座历史同 A. T. and S. 铁路一样悠久的古老乡间庄园，然后安安稳稳、从从容容地住下来。您可知道——在百万富翁里头，我这人脾气算相当好的，又喜欢交游，可整个纽约城，我没有六个真朋友。想想看吧！”

“我呢，”巴比伦说，“除了少年时代在洛桑（Lausanne）结交的朋友，一个朋友也没有。我在英国待了三十年，到头来除了一口纯正的英语，外加足以装满一只相当大的箱子的金币，别无所获。”

===Original===
These two plutocrats breathed a simultaneous sigh.

“Talking of gold coin,” said Racksole, “how much money should you think Jules has contrived to amass while he has been with you?”

“Oh!” Babylon smiled. “I should not like to guess. He has had unique opportunities﻿—opportunities.”

“Should you consider twenty thousand an extraordinary sum under the circumstances?”

“Not at all. Has he been confiding in you?”

===Chinese===
这两位大富豪不约而同地叹了口气。

“说起金币，”拉克索尔道，“你猜朱尔跟着你这些年，设法攒下了多少钱？”

“哦！”巴比伦微微一笑。“这我可不愿去猜。他享有的机会是独一份的——机会啊。”

“照眼下的情形，两万镑这个数目，你当它离奇吗？”

“一点也不。他向你交过底？”

===Original===
“Somewhat. I have dismissed him.”

“You have dismissed him?”

“Why not?”

“There is no reason why not. But I have felt inclined to dismiss him for the past ten years, and never found courage to do it.”

“It was a perfectly simple proceeding, I assure you. Before I had done with him, I rather liked the fellow.”

===Chinese===
“交了一点。我把他辞了。”

“你把他辞了？”

“有什么不可？”

“没有什么不可。只不过十年来我一直有意辞掉他，却始终没鼓起那份勇气。”

“我向您担保，手续简单之极。等跟他把这一场交道打完，我倒有点喜欢上这家伙了。”

===Original===
“Miss Spencer and Jules﻿—both gone in one day!” mused Félix Babylon.

“And no one to take their places,” said Racksole. “And yet the hotel continues its way!”

But when Racksole reached the Grand Babylon he found that Miss Spencer’s chair in the bureau was occupied by a stately and imperious girl, dressed becomingly in black.

“Heavens, Nella!” he cried, going to the bureau. “What are you doing here?”

“I am taking Miss Spencer’s place. I want to help you with your hotel, Dad. I fancy I shall make an excellent hotel clerk. I have arranged with a Miss Selina Smith, one of the typists in the office, to put me up to all the tips and tricks, and I shall do very well.”

===Chinese===
“斯宾塞小姐和朱尔——一天之内双双离去！”费利克斯·巴比伦沉吟道。

“又没有一个人来接替，”拉克索尔说，“可这饭店照样运转不误！”

然而拉克索尔回到大巴比伦时，却发现事务处（bureau）里斯宾塞小姐那把椅子上，坐了一位仪态雍容、顾盼生威的姑娘，一袭黑衣，穿得熨帖入时。

“天哪，内拉（Nella）！”他一边朝事务处走去一边喊道，“你在这儿做什么？”

“我来顶斯宾塞小姐的班呀。爸爸，你的饭店，我想帮你一把。我自信能当个呱呱叫的饭店文书。我跟办公室里一位打字员塞莉娜·史密斯小姐（Miss Selina Smith）说妥了，由她把这里的种种门道诀窍一一传授给我，我准能干得漂漂亮亮。”

===Original===
“But look here, Helen Racksole. We shall have the whole of London talking about this thing﻿—the greatest of all American heiresses a hotel clerk! And I came here for quiet and rest!”
